\Sigma_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}
,
\widehat{\Sigma}_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
respectively associated to
\rho
and
\widehat{\rho}
.

\widetilde{T}_{0}

\widetilde{T}_{i}

\widetilde{T}_{j}

x_{i}

y_{i}

z_{i}

x_{j}

y_{j}

z_{j}

u_{0}

v_{0}

w_{0}

x

y

Figure 7.
The setup of Proposition
23
.

As before, consider a family
\widetilde{T}_{1}
,
\widetilde{T}_{2}
, …,
\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}
of components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
u_{0}v_{0}
from
xy
, indexed in this order from
u_{0}v_{0}
to
xy
in the sense that each
\widetilde{T}_{i}
separates
u_{0}v_{0}
from
\widetilde{T}_{i+1}
. Index the vertices of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
as
x_{i}
,
y_{i}
,
z_{i}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
in such a way that they occur counterclockwise in this order around
\widetilde{T}_{i}
and that
x_{i}y_{i}
is the side of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
facing
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. See Figure
7
. For
i=1
,
2
, …,
i_{0}
, let
E_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{i})
,
F_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{i})
,
G_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{i})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the flags associated to the vertices of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
by the flag map
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}\colon\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}\to\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and similarly define
E_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(u_{0})
,
F_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(v_{0})
,
G_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w_{0})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

By Lemma
13
, there is a unique projective map
A_{0}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
sending the flag
E_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(u_{0})
to
\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(u_{0})
, the flag
F_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(v_{0})
to
\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(v_{0})
, and the line
G_{0}^{(1)}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w_{0})^{(1)}
to
\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(w_{0})^{(1)}
.

Proposition 23
.

The slithering map
\widehat{\Sigma}_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}
defined by
\widehat{\rho}
is the limit of

A_{0}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ\Sigma_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

as the family
\{\widetilde{T}_{1},\widetilde{T}_{2},\dots,\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}\}
tends to the set of all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
\widetilde{T}_{0}
from
\widetilde{T}
, where

A(\widetilde{T}_{i})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}F_{i}}(\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}\left(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\displaystyle\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}S^{a(b+c)}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(-\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }xy\text{ faces the side }x_{i}z_{i}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{i}\\
\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}F_{i}}(\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}R^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}\left(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\displaystyle\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}S^{(a+c)b}_{G_{i}F_{i}}(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{G_{i}F_{i}}(-\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }xy\text{ faces the side }y_{i}z_{i}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{i},\end{cases}

where
A_{0}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
is defined as above, where

\displaystyle L^{abc}_{EFG}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-t}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle R^{abc}_{EFG}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{e}^{t}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle S^{ab}_{EF}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{t}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

are the left eruption, right eruption and shearing maps of
§
3.3
and
§
3.4
, and where the variations
\Delta\sigma^{ab}
,
\Delta\tau^{abc}
of generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants are expressed using the framework of Lemmas
18
and
19
.

In §
3.3
and §
3.4
, we only introduced the maps
L^{abc}_{EFG}(t)
,
R^{abc}_{EFG}(t)
and
S^{ab}_{EF}(t)
as projective maps, but we are here considering them as actual linear maps
\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

In the definition of
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
, note that the commutativity properties of Lemmas
16
(5–6) and
17
(3) guarantee that the compositions
\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{EF}(\ )
,
\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L^{abc}_{EFG}(\ )
and
\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}R^{abc}_{EFG}(\ )
do not depend on the order in which their terms are taken. Also, although the element
A_{0}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
is only determined projectively, conjugating an element of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
by an element of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
gives a well-defined element of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, so that the formula given in the statement of Proposition
23
makes sense.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
23
will take a while, and we split it into several steps.

We first lift some of the mystery behind the formulas defining the terms
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
, by discussing the key property motivating these definitions.

The generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants are defined so that

\displaystyle\exp\tau_{\rho}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})

\displaystyle=X_{abc}(E_{i},F_{i},G_{i})

\displaystyle\exp\sigma_{\rho}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})

\displaystyle=X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\big(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(G_{0}),G_{i}\big)

for every
a
,
b
,
c
,
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n
(see §
3.2
and Fact
22
).

Moving from
\rho
to
\widehat{\rho}
, Lemmas
16
(4) and
17
(2) show that there exists a flag
G_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that

(1)

\displaystyle\exp\tau_{\widehat{\rho}}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})

\displaystyle=X_{abc}(E_{i},F_{i},G_{i}^{\prime})

(2)

\displaystyle\exp\sigma_{\widehat{\rho}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})

\displaystyle=X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(G_{0}),G_{i}^{\prime})

for every
a
,
b
,
c
,
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n
. In addition, the combination of Proposition
12
and Lemma
13
shows that
G_{i}^{\prime}
is unique.

Lemma 24
.

The map
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the unique unipotent isomorphism that sends the flag
G_{i}
to
G_{i}^{\prime}
, respects the flag
E_{i}
if
xy
faces the side
x_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
, and respects
F_{i}
if
xy
faces the side
y_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
.

Proof.

Consider the case where
xy
faces the side
x_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
. The other case is essentially identical.

All the terms
S_{E_{i}F_{i}}^{ab}
,
L_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
,
S_{E_{i}G_{i}}^{a^{\prime\prime}b^{\prime\prime}}
involved in the formula

\displaystyle A(\widetilde{T}_{i})

\displaystyle=\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}F_{i}}(\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}\left(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}S^{a(b+c)}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(-\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))

respect the flag
E_{i}
, by Lemmas
16
(1) and
17
(1). It follows that their composition
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
also respects
E_{i}
.

Similarly,

\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}S^{a(b+c)}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(-\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))(G_{i})=G_{i}.

If we set

G_{i}^{\prime\prime}=\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}\left(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\right)(G_{i}),

Lemma
16
(4) shows that

\displaystyle X_{abc}(E_{i},F_{i},G_{i}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})}X_{abc}(E_{i},F_{i},G_{i})

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\tau_{\rho}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})+\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\tau_{\widehat{\rho}}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})

for every
a
,
b
,
c\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=n
. Also, by Lemma
16
(3),
G_{i}
and
G_{i}^{\prime\prime}
have the same line
G_{i}^{(1)}=G_{i}^{\prime\prime(1)}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(G_{0}),G_{i}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(G_{0}),G_{i})

\displaystyle=\exp\sigma_{\rho}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})

for every
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}\geqslant 1
with
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n
.

Finally, the last term
\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}F_{i}}(\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))
respects
E_{i}
and
F_{i}
, and sends
G_{i}^{\prime\prime}
to a flag
G_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle X_{abc}(E_{i},F_{i},G_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=X_{abc}(E_{i},F_{i},G_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\exp\tau_{\widehat{\rho}}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})

\displaystyle X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(G_{0}),G_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\Delta\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})}X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(G_{0}),G_{i}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\sigma_{\rho}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})+\Delta\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\sigma_{\widehat{\rho}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})

for every
a
,
b
,
c
,
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n
, using Proposition
11
and Lemma
17
(2). Then
G_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime}=G_{i}^{\prime}
by Proposition
12
and Lemma
13
, and by definition of
G_{i}^{\prime}
in (
1
–
2
).

This completes the proof that
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
respects
E_{i}
and sends
G_{i}
to
G_{i}^{\prime}
.

There remains to prove that
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
is unipotent. For this, we will use a basis
\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}\}
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
that is adapted to the transverse flag pair
(E_{i},F_{i})
, in the sense that each space
E_{i}^{(a)}
is generated by the first
a
vectors
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
, …,
e_{a}
, and each
F_{i}^{(b)}
is spanned by the last
b
vectors
e_{n-b+1}
,
e_{n-b+2}
, …,
e_{n}
.

By its definition, the shearing map

S^{ab}_{E_{i}F_{i}}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{t}\mathrm{Id}_{E_{i}^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F_{i}^{(b)}}

is diagonal in this basis, with its first
a
entries equal to
\mathrm{e}^{t}
and the remaining ones equal to
1
.

The left eruption map

L^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}\left(t\right)=\mathrm{e}^{-t}\mathrm{Id}_{E_{i}^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F_{i}^{(b)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{G_{i}^{(c)}}

sends
e_{j}
to
\mathrm{e}^{-t}e_{j}
if
j\leqslant a
and sends
e_{j}
to
e_{j}
if
j\geqslant n-c+1
. For
a<j\leqslant n-c
, use the direct sum decomposition
\mathbb{R}^{n}=E_{i}^{(a)}\oplus F_{i}^{(b)}\oplus G_{i}^{(c)}
and write
e_{j}
as
e_{j}=v_{a}+v_{b}+v_{c}
with

v_{a}\in E_{i}^{(a)}
,
v_{b}\in F_{i}^{(b)}
and
v_{c}\in G_{i}^{(c)}
. Then

L^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}(t)(e_{j})=\mathrm{e}^{-t}v_{a}+v_{b}+v_{c}=(\mathrm{e}^{-t}-1)v_{a}+e_{j}.

Since
v_{a}\in E_{i}^{(a)}
is a linear combination of
e_{k}
with
k\leqslant a<j
, this proves that the matrix of
L^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}\left(t\right)
in the basis
\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}\}
is upper triangular, with its first
a
diagonal entries equal to
\mathrm{e}^{-t}
and the remaining diagonal entries equal to 1.

Finally, the shearing map

S^{ab}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(t)=\mathrm{e}^{t}\mathrm{Id}_{E_{i}^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{G_{i}^{(b)}}

sends
e_{j}
to
\mathrm{e}^{t}e_{j}
when
j\leqslant a
. When
j>a
, we again decompose
e_{j}
as
e_{j}=v_{a}+v_{b}
with
v_{a}\in E_{i}^{(a)}
and
v_{b}\in G_{i}^{(b)}
. Then

S^{ab}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(t)(e_{j})=\mathrm{e}^{t}v_{a}+v_{b}=(\mathrm{e}^{t}-1)v_{a}+e_{j}.

This again proves that
S^{ab}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(t)
is upper triangular in the basis
\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}\}
, with its first
a
diagonal entries equal to
\mathrm{e}^{t}
, the remaining diagonal entries equal to
1
.

If we apply these computations to each term in

\displaystyle A(\widetilde{T}_{i})

\displaystyle=\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}F_{i}}(\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L^{abc}_{E_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}\big(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}S^{a(b+c)}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{i}G_{i}}(-\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))

we see that the matrix of
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
in the basis
\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}\}
is upper diagonal, with its
j
–th diagonal entry equal to

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a\geqslant j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{e}^{\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a\geqslant j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a\geqslant j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{e}^{\Delta\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a\geqslant j\end{subarray}}\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})}=1.

Therefore,
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
is a unipotent isomorphism that respects the flag
E_{i}
and sends
G_{i}
to
G_{i}^{\prime}
. The unipotent condition guarantees that this map is unique. This completes the proof of Lemma
24
when
xy
faces the side
x_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
.

The proof in the other case, where
xy
faces the side
y_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
, is essentially identical.
∎

As in the construction of the slithering maps, let
x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}
be the side of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
that faces
xy
. In particular, either
x_{i}^{\prime}=x_{i}
and
y_{i}^{\prime}=z_{i}
, or
x_{i}^{\prime}=z_{i}
and
y_{i}^{\prime}=y_{i}
.

The next step in the proof of Proposition
23
is the following computation.

Lemma 25
.

\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}\circ\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right).

Proof.

Let us restrict attention to the case where
xy
faces the side
x_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
, namely where
x_{i}^{\prime}=x_{i}
and
y_{i}^{\prime}=z_{i}
. As usual, the other case will be almost identical.

Let

\Sigma=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}\circ\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)

denote the right hand side of the equation. Lemma
24
shows that
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
respects the flag
E_{i}
and is unipotent, and
\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}
also respects
E_{i}
and is unipotent by definition of slithering maps. It follows that
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}
respects
E_{i}
and is unipotent. Since
\Sigma
is conjugate to
A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}
, it therefore is also unipotent.

In an obvious adaptation of the notation to
\widehat{\rho}
, set
\widehat{E}_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(x_{i})
,
\widehat{F}_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(y_{i})
,
\widehat{G}_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(z_{i})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for
i=1
,
2
, …,
i_{0}
, and
\widehat{E}_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(u_{0})
,
\widehat{F}_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(v_{0})
,
\widehat{G}_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(w_{0})
. Similarly, let
E_{i}^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{i}^{\prime})
,
F_{i}^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{i}^{\prime})
,
\widehat{E}_{i}^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(x_{i}^{\prime})
,
\widehat{F}_{i}^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{\widehat{\rho}}(y_{i}^{\prime})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)(\widehat{E}_{i})

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}(\widehat{E}_{0})=\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}(E_{0})=E_{i}

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)(\widehat{F}_{i})

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}(\widehat{F}_{0})=\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}(F_{0})=F_{i}.

Since we are in the case where
xy
faces the side
x_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
,
E_{i}^{\prime}=E_{i}
and it follows that

\displaystyle\Sigma(\widehat{E}_{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}(E_{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})(E_{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}(E_{i})=\widehat{E}_{i}.

Finally, since
F_{i}^{\prime}=G_{i}
in the case considered,

\displaystyle\Sigma(\widehat{F}_{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}(F_{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})(G_{i})

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}(G_{i}^{\prime})

where the flag
G_{i}^{\prime}
is defined as in Lemma
24
.

Therefore, by application of the linear isomorphism
\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}
and remembering that the double-ratios of four flags depend only on the lines of the last two flags, the flag
\Sigma(\widehat{F}_{i})
is such that

\displaystyle X_{abc}\big(\widehat{E}_{i},\widehat{F}_{i},\Sigma(\widehat{F}_{i})\big)

\displaystyle=X_{abc}(E_{i},F_{i},G_{i}^{\prime})=\exp\tau_{\widehat{\rho}}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})

\displaystyle=X_{abc}\big(\widehat{E}_{i},\widehat{F}_{i},\widehat{G}_{i}\big)

\displaystyle X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\big(\widehat{E}_{i},\widehat{F}_{i};\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(\widehat{G}_{0}),\Sigma(\widehat{F}_{i})\big)

\displaystyle=X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\big(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}(\widehat{G}_{0}),G_{i}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle=X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\big(E_{i},F_{i};\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(G_{0}),G_{i}^{\prime}\big)=\exp\sigma_{\rho}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i})

\displaystyle=X_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\big(\widehat{E}_{i},\widehat{F}_{i};\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}(\widehat{G}_{0}),\widehat{G}_{i}\big)

for every
a
,
b
,
c
,
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n
. By Proposition
12
and Lemma
13
, this proves that
\Sigma(\widehat{F}_{i})=\widehat{G}_{i}=\widehat{F}_{i}^{\prime}
.

Therefore,
\Sigma
is a unipotent map respecting
\widehat{E}_{i}=\widehat{E}_{i}^{\prime}
and sending
\widehat{F}_{i}
to
\widehat{F}_{i}^{\prime}
. By uniqueness of such a map,
\Sigma
is equal to the slithering map
\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}
.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
25
in the case where
xy
faces the side
x_{i}z_{i}
of
\widetilde{T}_{i}
. The other case is essentially identical.
∎

Our next step is to borrow two estimates from
[
BD17
]
. To quantify the “width” of a component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, choose a geodesic arc
k\subset\widetilde{S}
joining a point of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
to a point of
xy
. Then, for every component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, we can consider the length
\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})
of its intersection with
k
. Let
\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}^{\phantom{\prime}}y_{\widetilde{T}}^{\phantom{\prime}},x_{\widetilde{T}}^{\prime}y_{\widetilde{T}}^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
be the slithering map associated to such a component, where
x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}}
is the side of
\widetilde{T}
that faces
\widetilde{T}_{0}
and where
x_{\widetilde{T}}^{\prime}y_{\widetilde{T}}^{\prime}
is the side facing
xy
.

Lemma 26
.

There exists a number
\nu>0
such that

\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}^{\phantom{\prime}}y_{\widetilde{T}}^{\phantom{\prime}},x_{\widetilde{T}}^{\prime}y_{\widetilde{T}}^{\prime}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+O\left(\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})^{\nu}\right)

for every component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

In addition, the exponent
\nu
and the constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O\left(\ \right)
depend only on a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
containing the arc
k
, and on a compact subset containing
\rho
in the space of Hitchin representations (assuming the geodesic lamination
\lambda
given).

Proof.

See
[
BD17
, Lem. 5.2]
.
∎

Lemma 27
.

As
\widetilde{T}
ranges over all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that separate
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, the sum

\sum_{\widetilde{T}}\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})^{\nu}

is convergent for every
\nu>0
.

More precisely, for every
\nu>\nu^{\prime}>0
,

\sum_{\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})<\varepsilon}\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})^{\nu}=O(\varepsilon^{\nu^{\prime}})

for every
\varepsilon>0
, where the sum is taken over all
\widetilde{T}
with
\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})<\varepsilon
and where the constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O(\ )
depends only on a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
containing the arc
k
and on the exponents
\nu
,
\nu^{\prime}
.

Proof.

See
[
BD17
, Lem. 5.3]
.
∎

Lemma 28
.

For the exponent
\nu
of Lemma
26
,

A(\widetilde{T}_{i})=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+O\left(\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T}_{i})^{\nu}\right)

where the constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O(\ )
depends only on a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
containing the arc
k
, and on a compact subset of the space of Hitchin representations containing both
\rho
and
\widehat{\rho}
.

Proof.

We can rewrite the equality of Lemma
25
as

A(\widetilde{T}_{i})=\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}\circ\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}.

By Lemma
26
,
\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+O\left(\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T}_{i})^{\nu}\right)
.

Also, the slithering map
\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}
is defined as the (infinite) composition of the elementary slithering maps
\Sigma_{\widetilde{T}}
associated to the components
\widetilde{T}
separating
x_{i}y_{i}
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. By the combination of Lemmas
26
and
27
, it follows that
\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
. The same holds for
\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}
,
\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}
and
\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}
. Therefore, the terms
\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}
and their inverses are uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
. it follows that

\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}\circ\left(\Sigma_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}^{-1}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{u_{0}v_{0},x_{i}y_{i}}\right)^{-1}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+O\left(\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T}_{i})^{\nu}\right).

Since
\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},x_{i}y_{i}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+O\left(\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T}_{i})^{\nu}\right)
by another application of Lemma
26
, this concludes the proof of Lemma
28
.
∎

We now return to the proof of Proposition
23
.
By another manipulation of the equality of Lemma
25
,

A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}=\left(A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}y_{i},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}\right)\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i}).

Similarly, let
B_{i}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
be such that

A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}=\left(A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}\right)\circ B_{i}.

Namely,

B_{i}=\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}.

Then,

A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}=B_{0}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ B_{1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ B_{2}\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}}

if we define

\displaystyle B_{0}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{1}y_{1},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{x_{1}y_{1},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}

\displaystyle B_{i_{0}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{x_{i_{0}}^{\prime}y_{i_{0}}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i_{0}}^{\prime}y_{i_{0}}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}.

Lemma 29
.

As the family
\{\widetilde{T}_{1},\widetilde{T}_{2},\dots,\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}\}
tends to the set of all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, the two terms

B_{0}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ B_{1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ B_{2}\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}}

and

A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})

have the same limit.

Proof.

We first rewrite

\displaystyle B_{i}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}\right)\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\circ\left(\Sigma_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime},u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\circ\Sigma_{x_{i+1}y_{i+1},x_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}}^{-1}.

The same argument as in the proof of Lemma
28
then gives that

B_{i}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+O\left(\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})^{\nu}\right)

where
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
ranges over all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that separate
\widetilde{T}_{i}
from
\widetilde{T}_{i+1}
, if
i<i_{0}
, and over all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that separate
\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}
from
xy
if
i=i_{0}
.

Now, consider

C_{i}=A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ B_{i}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i+1})\circ B_{i+1}\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}},

obtained from

C_{0}=B_{0}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ B_{1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ B_{2}\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}}

by omitting the
B_{j}
with
j<i
. Then, using Lemmas
28
and
27
as well as the above estimate for the
B_{j}
to bound the terms
A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})
and
A(\widetilde{T}_{i+1})\circ B_{i+1}\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}}
,

\displaystyle C_{i}-C_{i+1}

\displaystyle=A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i})\circ(B_{i}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i+1})\circ B_{i+1}\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}}

\displaystyle=O\left(\sum_{T}\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})^{\nu}\right)

where the sum is over all the components
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that separate
\widetilde{T}_{i}
from
\widetilde{T}_{i+1}
.

By induction, we conclude that

C_{0}-C_{i_{0}}=O\left(\sum_{T\not\in\mathcal{T}}\ell(k\cap\widetilde{T})^{\nu}\right)

where the sum is now over all the components
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that separate
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
and do not belong to the family
\mathcal{T}=\{\widetilde{T}_{1},\widetilde{T}_{2},\dots,\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}\}
. By Lemma
27
,
C_{0}-C_{i_{0}}
therefore converges to 0 as the family
\mathcal{T}
tends to the set of all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. Since

\displaystyle C_{0}

\displaystyle=B_{0}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ B_{1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}}

\displaystyle C_{i_{0}}

\displaystyle=A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}),

this completes the proof of Lemma
29
.
∎

Because

B_{0}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ B_{1}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ B_{i_{0}}=A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}

is actually independent of the family
\mathcal{T}=\{\widetilde{T}_{1},\widetilde{T}_{2},\dots,\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}\}
, this shows that
A_{0}^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Sigma}_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}\circ\Sigma_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}^{-1}
is the limit of
A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})
as
\mathcal{T}
tends to the set of all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

Equivalently,
\widehat{\Sigma}_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}
is the limit of

A_{0}\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ\Sigma_{xy,u_{0}v_{0}}\circ A_{0}^{-1}

as the family
\{\widetilde{T}_{1},\widetilde{T}_{2},\dots,\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}\}
tends to the set of all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
xy
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. This completes the proof of Proposition
23
.
∎

4.4.
Infinitesimal variation of flag maps

Let
t\mapsto[\rho_{t}]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
be a smooth curve in the Hitchin component, tangent to a vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
at the point
[\rho]=[\rho_{0}]
. Our overall goal is to describe the Weil class
[c_{V}]\in H^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
of this tangent vector
V
in terms of the directional derivatives

\displaystyle\dot{\tau}^{abc}_{V}(T,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\tau^{abc}_{[\rho_{t}]}(T,x)_{|t=0}

\displaystyle\dot{\sigma}^{ab}_{V}(e)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\sigma^{ab}_{[\rho_{t}]}(e)_{|t=0}

of its generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants. In order to implement the methods of §
2.2
, we will rely on the computations of §
4.3
to determine the corresponding infinitesimal variation of the flag map
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}\colon\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}\to\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, namely the tangent vector

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(w)_{|t=0}\in T_{\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w)}\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

for
w\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
. Actually, we will not need this computation for all
w\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
, just for those
w
in the dense subset consisting of all vertices of the components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
.

The formulas of §
4.3
involved the eruption and shearing maps

\displaystyle L^{abc}_{EFG}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-t}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}

\displaystyle R^{abc}_{EFG}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{e}^{t}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}

\displaystyle S^{ab}_{EF}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{t}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}.

While these were well-defined linear isomorphisms
\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
in §
4.3
, it is now convenient to resume considering them projectively, as elements of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, and to take the derivatives

(3)

\displaystyle\dot{L}^{abc}_{EFG}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}L^{abc}_{EFG}(t)_{|t=0}=\tfrac{-b-c}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\tfrac{a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\tfrac{a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}\in\mathfrak{pgl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})=\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

(4)

\displaystyle\dot{R}^{abc}_{EFG}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}R^{abc}_{EFG}(t)_{|t=0}=\tfrac{-b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\tfrac{a+c}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\tfrac{-b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

(5)

\displaystyle\dot{S}^{ab}_{EF}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}S^{ab}_{EF}(t)_{|t=0}=\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\tfrac{-a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}).

Note that, in this context, one needs to rescale the original formulas to represent
L^{abc}_{EFG}(t)
,
R^{abc}_{EFG}(t)
,
S^{ab}_{EF}(t)\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
by elements of
\mathrm{SL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, in order to take advantage of the isomorphism between the Lie algebras
\mathfrak{pgl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
.

For a flag
F\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\dot{A}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, let

\dot{A}F=\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}F_{|t=0}\in T_{F}\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

denote the vector tangent to the curve
t\mapsto A_{t}F\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
defined by any curve
t\mapsto A_{t}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
tangent to
\dot{A}=\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}{}_{|t=0}
at
A_{0}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
.

To simplify the formulas, we normalize the Hitchin representations
\rho_{t}\colon\pi_{1}(S)\to\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
as follows. Arbitrarily choose a base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
among all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
. Index its vertices as
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
counterclockwise in this order around
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. By Lemma
13
, there is a projective map
A_{t}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
sending the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
, and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
. After conjugating
\rho_{t}
with
A_{t}
, which replaces the flag map
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}
with
A_{t}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}
, we can therefore arrange that the flags
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}\in\mathbb{RP}^{n-1}
are independent of
t
, namely respectively equal to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
.

Finally, for every component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that is different from the base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, we counterclockwise index the vertices of
\widetilde{T}
as
x_{\widetilde{T}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
in such a way that
x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}}
is the side of
\widetilde{T}
that faces
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. Then, for each component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
(including
\widetilde{T}_{0}
), we consider the flags
E_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}})
,
F_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}})
,
G_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Proposition 30
.

Let
\widetilde{T}
be a component of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that is distinct from the base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. Then, for every vertex
w\in\{x_{\widetilde{T}},y_{\widetilde{T}},z_{\widetilde{T}}\}
of
\widetilde{T}
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(w)_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\dot{B}(\widetilde{T}_{0})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w)+\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w)+\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w)

where the sum is over all components
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
\widetilde{T}_{0}
from
\widetilde{T}
, where

\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}^{abc}_{V}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{(a+c)b}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}\text{ faces the side }y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime},\end{cases}

where

\dot{B}(\widetilde{T}_{0})=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\widetilde{T}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{0}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}&\text{if }\widetilde{T}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{0}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}&\text{if }\widetilde{T}\text{ faces the side }y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{0},\end{cases}

and where

\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }w=x_{\widetilde{T}}\\
0&\text{if }w=y_{\widetilde{T}}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}}+\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}\\
\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}}+\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}&\text{if }w=z_{\widetilde{T}}.\end{cases}

Proof.

As in Proposition
23
, index the vertices of the base triangle
\widetilde{T}_{0}
as
u_{0}
,
v_{0}
,
w_{0}
, clockwise in this order, in such a way that
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
u_{0}v_{0}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

We clearly need to distinguish cases. Let us first focus on the case where
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. In particular,
u_{0}=y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
and
v_{0}=x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
.

Let us apply Proposition
23
to compute the slithering map
\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}
associated to
\widehat{\rho}=\rho_{t}
. Since
u_{0}=y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
and
v_{0}=x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
, the map
A_{0}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
occurring in the formula is equal to the identity, by our normalization that the flag map
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}
respects the flags
E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
and the line
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}
.

Let
\widetilde{T}_{1}
,
\widetilde{T}_{2}
, …,
\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}
be a family of components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
\widetilde{T}
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, indexed in this order from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
to
\widetilde{T}
. Then, if
\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}=\tau^{abc}_{\rho_{t}}-\tau^{abc}_{\rho}
and
\Delta_{t}\sigma^{ab}=\sigma^{ab}_{\rho_{t}}-\sigma^{ab}_{\rho}
denote the variations of generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants, Proposition
23
asserts that the slithering map
\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}
is the limit of

A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\circ\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

as the family
\{\widetilde{T}_{1},\widetilde{T}_{2},\dots,\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}\}
tends to the set of all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
\widetilde{T}
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, where

A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{i})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}(\Delta_{t}\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L^{abc}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}\left(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\displaystyle\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}S^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}(-\Delta_{t}\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}\text{ faces the side }x_{i}z_{i}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{i}\\
\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}(\Delta_{t}\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}R^{abc}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}\left(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i})\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\displaystyle\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}S^{(a+c)b}_{G_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{i},x_{i}))\circ\Bigcirc_{a+b=n}S^{ab}_{G_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}}(-\Delta_{t}\sigma^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{i}))\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}\text{ faces the side }y_{i}z_{i}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{i}.\end{cases}

Now, the constructions of
[
BD17
]
and the proof of Proposition
23
admit automatic holomorphic extensions if we allow the variations
\Delta\tau^{abc}
and
\Delta\sigma^{ab}
of generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants to be complex with a small enough real part, as in
[
Bon96
]
for the case
n=2
and
[
MMMZ23
]
for any
n
. The existence of this holomorphic extension enables us to take the derivative term-by-term in the limit and to prove that, for every flag
F\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, the tangent vector

\frac{d}{dt}\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}(F)_{|t=0}\in T_{\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}(F)}\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

is equal to the limit of

\left(\sum_{i=1}^{i_{0}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}_{i})\right)\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}(F)

as the family
\{\widetilde{T}_{1},\widetilde{T}_{2},\dots,\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}}\}
tends to the set of all components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
\widetilde{T}
from the base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, where

\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}_{i})=\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{i})_{|t=0}.

An application of the chain rule shows that
\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}_{i})
is as indicated in the statement of Proposition
30
.

We can rephrase this property by saying that

\frac{d}{dt}\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}(F)_{|t=0}=\left(\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\right)\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}(F)

for every flag
F\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

We now consider the subcase where the vertex
w
is equal to
x_{\widetilde{T}}
. We can then apply the above conclusion to
F=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(u_{0})
. Since we are in the case where
u_{0}=y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
, the flag
F
is also equal to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(u_{0})
for every
t
by our normalization convention for the Hitchin representations
\rho_{t}
. Then, by definition of the slithering map,

\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}(F)=\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}\big(\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(u_{0})\big)=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}}).

The special case
t=0
yields

\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}(F)=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}}).

Reporting these values into our earlier computation, we obtain that

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}})_{|t=0}=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}}).

This proves Proposition
30
in the case where
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
and
w=x_{\widetilde{T}}
.

Considering the flag
F=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(v_{0})=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
instead similarly provides the proof of Proposition
30
when
w=y_{\widetilde{T}}
, still assuming that
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

When
w=z_{\widetilde{T}}
, we apply Proposition
23
to the leaf
x_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
and take term-by-term derivatives in the limit as above. After observing that
\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}\big(\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(v_{0})\big)=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}})
, we conclude that

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}})_{|t=0}=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})+\dot{A}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})

with

\displaystyle\dot{A}(\widetilde{T})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\left(\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}\right).

By construction, the shearing map
S^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}(t)\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
respects
G_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})
. Therefore,
\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})=0
and the term
\dot{A}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})
is equal to
\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})
with

\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}}

as in the statement of Proposition
30
. (Note that, although
\dot{A}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})=\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})
, the terms
\dot{A}(\widetilde{T})
,
\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
are usually different.) This shows that

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}})_{|t=0}=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})+\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})

as in Proposition
30
.

Now, we could have used the side
y_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
of
\widetilde{T}
instead of
x_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
. This would lead us to the same statement as above, but with

\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}}.

This proves the equality of the two expressions for
\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})
given in the statement of Proposition
30
(which can also be tracked down to the identity
L^{abc}_{EFG}(G)=R^{abc}_{EFG}(G)
of Lemma
16
).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
30
in the case where
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

Let us now consider the case where
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
. In this case,
u_{0}=x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
and
v_{0}=z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
. The formula for
\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}
provided by Proposition
23
then involves the unique projective map
A_{0}^{t}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
sending the flag
E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
, the flag
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
, and the line
F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
to
F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t\kern 1.63885pt(1)}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
.

By definition of Fock-Goncharov invariants and since
E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}=E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
and
F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}=F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
, the flag
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}
is such that

X_{abc}(E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}},F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}},G^{t}_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})=\exp\tau_{\rho_{t}}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{0})

for every
a
,
b
,
c\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=n
. In addition, the line
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t\kern 1.63885pt(1)}
coincides with
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}
by our normalization of the flag maps
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}
.

On the other hand, Lemma
16
(4) shows that the composition

\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}\big(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{0})\big)

also sends
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
to a flag
\widehat{G}_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}
such that

X_{abc}(E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}},F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}},\widehat{G}^{t}_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})=\exp\tau_{\rho_{t}}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{0})

for every
a
,
b
,
c\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=n
. In addition, the line
\widehat{G}_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t\kern 1.63885pt(1)}
coincides with
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}
by Lemma
16
(3). It then immediately follows from Proposition
11
and Lemma
13
that
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}=\widehat{G}_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{t}
.

Since, by construction, the above composition of left eruptions also leaves
E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
invariant, as well as the line
F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}
, this proves that

A_{0}^{t}=\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}\big(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{0})\big).

In the case where
w=x_{\widetilde{T}}
,

\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}})=\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}\big(\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(u_{0})\big)=\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}\circ A_{0}^{t}\big(\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(u_{0})\big).

Proposition
23
then shows that
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}})
is the limit of

A_{0}^{t}\circ A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{1})\circ A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{2})\circ\dots\circ A_{t}(\widetilde{T}_{i_{0}})\big(\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}})\big).

Taking the derivative term-by-term in the limit as before, we conclude that

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}})_{|t=0}=\dot{B}(\widetilde{T}_{0})\big(\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}})\big)+\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}})

with

\dot{B}(\widetilde{T}_{0})=\frac{d}{dt}A_{0}^{t}{}_{|t=0}=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}.

This proves Proposition
30
in this subcase, where
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
and
w=x_{\widetilde{T}}
.

The argument is essentially identical in the subcase when
w=y_{\widetilde{T}}
, using now the fact that
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}})=\Sigma_{x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}},u_{0}v_{0}}^{t}\circ A_{0}^{t}\big(\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(v_{0})\big)
.

In the subcase where
w=z_{\widetilde{T}}
, the same analysis as in the case where
\widetilde{T}
faces
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
provides an additional term

\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})_{|t=0}=\dot{A}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})=\dot{B}(\widetilde{T})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})

as in the statement of Proposition
30
.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
30
in the case where
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

The proof when
\widetilde{T}
faces the side
y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
is essentially identical.
∎

5.
The Weil cohomology class associated to a tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)

We now have all the analytic tools needed to implement the framework of §
2.2
, and express the Weil cohomology class
[c_{V}]\in H^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
associated to a tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
as a simplicial cohomology class for a suitable triangulation of the surface
S
. As in §
4.4
, we represent
V
as the vector tangent to a curve
t\mapsto[\rho_{t}]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
at
[\rho]=[\rho_{0}]
, and we consider the directional derivatives

\displaystyle\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(T,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\tau^{abc}_{[\rho_{t}]}(T,x)_{|t=0}

\displaystyle\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(e)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\sigma^{ab}_{[\rho_{t}]}(e)_{|t=0}

of the corresponding generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants. We also normalize the Hitchin representations
\rho_{t}
so that, at the vertices of the base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, the flags
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}\in\mathbb{RP}^{n-1}
are independent of
t
.

5.1.
Barriers for the geodesic lamination
\lambda

Consider a triangulation
\Sigma
of the surface
S
, which we lift to a triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
of the universal cover
\widetilde{S}
. As in §
2.2
, we want to define for each vertex
\widetilde{v}
of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
a projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})
such that

(1)

the choice of
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})
is
\rho_{t}
–equivariant, in the sense that
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\gamma\widetilde{v})=\rho_{t}(\gamma)\left(\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})\right)
for every
\gamma\in\pi_{1}(S)
and every vertex
\widetilde{v}
of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
;

(2)

for every vertex
\widetilde{v}
, the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})
depends differentiably on
t
.

For this, following an idea of Sun-Zhang
[
SZ17
]
, we introduce additional data for the geodesic lamination
\lambda
and impose a very mild transversality condition for the triangulation
\Sigma
.

The additional data is the following. In each connected component
T
of
S-\lambda
, we choose a Y-shaped
barrier
that is the union
\beta_{T}
of a point
\pi_{T}
in the ideal triangle
T
and of three infinite curves that respectively go from
\pi_{T}
to each of the vertices at infinity of
T
; for instance, these three arms of
\beta_{T}
can be the geodesics joining
\pi_{T}
to the vertices of
T
. Lift all these barriers
\beta_{T}
to barriers
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
for the components
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
. See Figure
8
.

\beta_{\widetilde{T}}

\beta_{\widetilde{T}}

x_{\widetilde{T}}

y_{\widetilde{T}}

\widetilde{v}

Figure 8.
A barrier in a component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}

We then require that each vertex of the triangulation
\Sigma
is disjoint from the geodesic lamination
\lambda
and its barriers
\beta_{T}
. This condition is easily attained by a small perturbation of
\Sigma
.

We can now use this data to define the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})
associated to each vertex
\widetilde{v}
of the triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
. By construction,
\widetilde{v}
is contained in a component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
and is disjoint from its barrier
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
. This barrier splits
\widetilde{T}
into three smaller triangles, each of which is delimited by one side of
\widetilde{T}
and by two arms of the barrier
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
. This naturally associates to
\widetilde{v}
a side of
\widetilde{T}
. Let
x_{\widetilde{v}}
,
y_{\widetilde{v}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
be the two endpoints of this side, and let
z_{\widetilde{v}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
be the third vertex of
\widetilde{T}
, choosing the indexing so that
x_{\widetilde{v}}
,
y_{\widetilde{v}}
and
z_{\widetilde{v}}
occur in this order counterclockwise around
\widetilde{T}
. See Figure
8
.

The projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})=\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}\}
is then defined as the unique projective basis such that:

(1)

the flag
\mathcal{F}_{t}(x_{\widetilde{v}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is the ascending flag of this basis, in the sense that the linear subspace
\mathcal{F}_{t}(x_{\widetilde{v}})^{(a)}
is spanned by
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
, …,
e_{a}
, for every
a=1
,
2
, …,
n
;

(2)

the flag
\mathcal{F}_{t}(y_{\widetilde{v}})
is its descending flag, in the sense that
\mathcal{F}_{t}(y_{\widetilde{v}})^{(b)}
is spanned by
e_{n-b+1}
,
e_{n-b+2}
, …,
e_{n}
, for every
b=1
,
2
, …,
n
;

(3)

the line
\mathcal{F}_{t}(z_{\widetilde{v}})^{(1)}
is spanned by the vector
e_{1}+e_{2}+\dots+e_{n}
.

Proposition
30
now provides us with a computation of the derivative

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(w)_{|t=0}\in T_{\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w)}\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

for each vertex
w\in\{x_{\widetilde{v}},y_{\widetilde{v}},z_{\widetilde{v}}\}
of
\widetilde{T}
. This will enable us to determine

c^{0}(\widetilde{v})=\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v})^{-1}_{|t=0}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}).

Interpreting
c^{0}
as a 0–cocycle
c^{0}\in C^{0}(\widetilde{S};\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
for the simplicial cohomology associated to the triangulation
\Sigma
, Lemma
10
shows that the Weil class
[c_{V}]\in H^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
associated to the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
is represented by the 1–cocycle
c_{V}=dc^{0}\in C^{1}(\widetilde{S};\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
. Namely, for every oriented edge
\widetilde{k}
of the triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
going from the vertex
\widetilde{v}_{-}
to the vertex
\widetilde{v}_{+}
,

c_{V}(\widetilde{k})=\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}_{|t=0}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}).

5.2.
A triangulation adapted to a train track neighborhood

Let
\Phi
be a train track neighborhood of
\lambda
and let
\Psi
be its associated train track, as in §
1.2
. In order to connect the computational scheme outlined in the previous section to the generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants associated to
\Psi
, we will use a triangulation
\Sigma
of the surface
S
that is well adapted to
\Phi
. This will be particularly convenient to compute the cup-product occurring in §
2.1
for the definition of the Atiyah-Bott-Goldman symplectic form.

Given the train track neighborhood
\Phi
, we can choose the barrier
\beta_{T}
so that each point where it enters
\Phi
is located very close to a corner of the complement
S-\Phi
, corresponding to a switch of
\Phi
; see Figure
6
in §
1.2
. To simplify subsequent exposition, we can even arrange that this entry point is systematically located in the branch incoming on the left side at that switch. See Figure
9
.

We then decompose each branch of
\Phi
into rectangles by splitting it along finitely many ties, including the switch ties at the end of the branch. Finally, we obtain a triangulation of
\Phi
, with all vertices on its boundary, by subdividing each rectangle into two triangles meeting along an arbitrary diagonal of the rectangle.

We need to be a little careful in the description of this triangulation of
\Phi
near a switch. First of all, the first rectangle of the outgoing branch at that point is actually a pentagon, because of the additional vertex coming from the corner where the two incoming branches meet; this pentagon therefore needs two additional edges decomposing it into three triangles. To ease the exposition, we decide to choose these two edges so that they are disjoint from this additional vertex. See Figure
9
.

Also, by our convention, the last rectangle of the branch incoming on the left at a switch meets a barrier
\beta_{T}
. Again to simplify further exposition, we decide to split this rectangle along a diagonal that is disjoint from the part of
\beta_{T}
that is near the switch. See Figure
9
.

\beta_{T}

\Phi

\Phi

\Phi

Figure 9.
A triangulation adapted to the train track neighborhood
\Phi

Finally, we extend this triangulation of the train track neighborhood
\Phi
to a triangulation
\Sigma
of the whole surface
S
. We choose the triangulation so that its vertices are disjoint from the barriers, and so that the triangulation is fine enough that every edge that is not contained in
\Phi
meets the barriers
\beta_{T}
in at most one point.

A key feature of this triangulation is that almost every face has at least one side that is disjoint from the geodesic lamination
\lambda
and from the barriers
\beta_{T}
. The only exceptions are the faces that contain the centers
\pi_{T}
of the barriers
\beta_{T}
, plus two faces near each switch. This property will come in handy when we compute cup-products in §
6
.

5.3.
Evaluation of the Weil cocycle on the edges of the triangulation

Lift the triangulation
\Sigma
that we just constructed to a triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
of the universal cover
\widetilde{S}
. It was specially designed so that, for the cocycle
c_{V}\in C^{1}(\widetilde{S};\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
representing the Weil class
[c_{V}]\in H^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
that we constructed in §
5.1
, the evaluation of
c_{V}
over the oriented edges of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is relatively simple.

Many edges
\widetilde{k}
of this triangulation are disjoint from the geodesic lamination
\widetilde{\lambda}
and from the barriers
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
. If, for an arbitrary orientation,
\widetilde{v}_{+}
and
\widetilde{v}_{-}
are the positive and negative endpoints of such an edge, the projective bases
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{\pm})
constructed in §
5.1
coincide and it follows that

c_{V}(\widetilde{k})=\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}_{|t=0}=0.

For future reference, we repeat this as follows.

Lemma 31
.

If
\widetilde{k}
is an oriented edge of the triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
that is disjoint from the geodesic lamination
\widetilde{\lambda}
and from the barriers
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
, then
c_{V}(\widetilde{k})=0
. ∎

The next simplest case is that of an oriented edge
\widetilde{k}
of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
that is disjoint from the geodesic lamination
\widetilde{\lambda}
but meets a barrier
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
. By construction of the triangulation,
\widetilde{k}
is contained in the closure of the complement of the train track neighborhood
\Phi
, and it meets
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
in exactly one point.

This case splits into two subcases according to whether
\widetilde{k}
turns to the left or to the right with respect to
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
. More precisely, let
\widetilde{v}_{+}
and
\widetilde{v}_{-}
be the positive and negative endpoints of
\widetilde{k}
. As in §
5.1
, the component of
\widetilde{T}-\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
that contains the negative endpoint
\widetilde{v}_{-}
of
\widetilde{k}
is adjacent to a side
x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}}
of
\widetilde{T}
, whose orientation is the boundary orientation coming from
\widetilde{T}
. Let
z_{\widetilde{T}}
denote the third vertex of
\widetilde{T}
. Then, either the positive endpoint
\widetilde{v}_{+}
is in the component of
\widetilde{T}-\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
that is adjacent to
x_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
, in which case we say that
\widetilde{k}
turns to the left
, or
\widetilde{v}_{+}
is in the component of
\widetilde{T}-\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
that is adjacent to
y_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
and
\widetilde{k}
turns to the right
. See Figure
10
.

\widetilde{k}

\widetilde{k}^{\prime}

Figure 10.
The oriented arcs
\widetilde{k}
and
\widetilde{k}^{\prime}
respectively turn left and right.

Lemma 32
.

If the oriented edge
\widetilde{k}
of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is disjoint from the geodesic lamination
\widetilde{\lambda}
and meets the barrier
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
in exactly one point,

c_{V}(\widetilde{k})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}&\text{ if }\widetilde{k}\text{ turns to the left}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}&\text{ if }\widetilde{k}\text{ turns to the right}\end{cases}

with
E_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}})
,
F_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}})
,
G_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Proof of Lemma
32
in a special case.

First consider the very special case where
\widetilde{T}
is the base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
used to define the bases
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})
, and the vertices
x_{\widetilde{T}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}}
are respectively equal to the vertices
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
. We will then use the fact that we have normalized the Hitchin representations
\rho_{t}
so that the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
are independent of
t
, and therefore respectively equal to
E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
and
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}
.

In particular, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is such that

X_{abc}\big(E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}},F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}},\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\big)=\exp\tau^{abc}_{\rho_{t}}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})

for every
a
,
b
,
c\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=n
. In addition, the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
is equal to
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}
. From the combination of Proposition
11
and Lemma
13
,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
is completely determined by these two properties. Lemma
16
(3–4) shows that the flag
\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}\big(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\big)(G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
satisfies the same properties. It follows that, for the variation
\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
of the triangle invariant,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})

\displaystyle=\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}\big(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\big)(G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}R_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}\big(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\big)(G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}),

where the second equality follows from Lemma
16
(2).

We are now ready to compute

c_{V}(\widetilde{k})=\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}_{|t=0}.

By definition, the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})
is the one for which
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
is the ascending flag,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
is the descending flag, and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
is spanned by the sum of the basis vectors. Since these objects are all independent of
t
, so is the basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})
, and

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}_{|t=0}=0.

For the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})
, we need to distinguish whether
\widetilde{k}
turns left or right, although these two subcases are not fundamentally different.

If
\widetilde{k}
turns left,
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})
is the projective basis for which
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
is the ascending flag,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
is the descending flag, and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
is spanned by the sum of the basis vectors. Set

B_{t}=\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}L_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}\big(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\big).

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})

\displaystyle=B_{t}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})=B_{t}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}=B_{t}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}

where the first equality is just a rephrasing of our earlier computation, the second and fourth equalities are consequences of our normalization, and the remaining two come from the properties of left eruptions stated in Lemma
16
(1–2). It follows that
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})=B_{t}\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})
, and

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}_{|t=0}=\frac{d}{dt}B_{t}{}_{|t=0}=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle c_{V}(\widetilde{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}

in this subcase where
\widetilde{k}
turns to the left, and
\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{T}_{0}
,
x_{\widetilde{T}}=x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}}=y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}}=z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
. This proves Lemma
32
in this subcase.

The argument is very similar for the subcase when
\widetilde{k}
turns to the right, in which case
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})=C_{t}\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})
with

C_{t}=\Bigcirc_{a+b+c=n}R_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}\big(\Delta_{t}\tau^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{0},x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})\big).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
32
in the special case where
\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{T}_{0}
,
x_{\widetilde{T}}=x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}}=y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}}=z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
32
in the general case.

In the general case, consider the unique projective map
A_{t}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
sending the flag
E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
E_{\widetilde{T}}^{t}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}})
, the flag
F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
F_{\widetilde{T}}^{t}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}})
, and the line
G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
to
G_{\widetilde{T}}^{t\kern 0.81949pt(1)}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}})^{(1)}
, as provided by Lemma
13
. We then use
A_{t}
to conjugate
\rho_{t}
to another representative
\rho_{t}^{\prime}\colon\pi_{1}(S)\to\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
of the Hitchin character
[\rho_{t}]=[\rho_{t}^{\prime}]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
, defined by the property that

\rho_{t}^{\prime}(\gamma)=A_{t}^{-1}\rho_{t}(\gamma)A_{t}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

for every
\gamma\in\pi_{1}(S)
.

The flag map of this new Hitchin representation
\rho_{t}^{\prime}
is then
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}^{\prime}}=A_{t}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}
, if we use the same symbols to denote
A_{t}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
and its action on
\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. As a consequence, the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v})
associated to each vertex
\widetilde{v}
by the flag map
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}^{\prime}}
is equal to the image
A_{t}^{-1}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})
of the earlier basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v})
under the action of
A_{t}^{-1}
. We can then use this new projective basis to compute

\displaystyle c_{V}(\widetilde{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}{}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}{}_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}A_{0}^{-1}{}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}A_{0}^{-1}{}_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}A_{0}^{-1}{}_{|t=0}+\frac{d}{dt}A_{0}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}A_{0}^{-1}{}_{|t=0}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{d}{dt}A_{t}A_{0}^{-1}{}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}A_{0}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}A_{0}^{-1}{}_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=A_{0}\left(\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}{}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}{}_{|t=0}\right)A_{0}^{-1}.

The flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}^{\prime}}(x_{\widetilde{T}})=E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}^{\prime}}(y_{\widetilde{T}})=F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}^{\prime}}(z_{\widetilde{T}})^{(1)}=G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}^{(1)}
are independent of
t
. We can therefore apply the special case to this new normalization
\rho_{t}^{\prime}
of the Hitchin representations representing the characters
[\rho_{t}]=[\rho_{t}^{\prime}]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
. For instance, when
\widetilde{k}
turns to the left, this gives

\displaystyle c_{V}(\widetilde{k})

\displaystyle=A_{0}\left(\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}{}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}{}_{|t=0}\right)A_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=A_{0}\left(\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{v}_{-}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}}^{abc}\right)A_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{v}_{-}})\dot{L}_{(A_{0}E_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})(A_{0}F_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})(A_{0}G_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})}^{abc}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{v}_{-}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}.

This proves the lemma when
\widetilde{k}
turns to the left.

The argument is identical when
\widetilde{k}
turns to the right, by replacing left eruptions with right eruptions, and concludes the proof of Lemma
32
.
∎

Lemmas
31
and
32
take care of all the edges of the triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
that are not contained in the train track neighborhood
\widetilde{\Phi}
. Among the edges of the triangulations of
\widetilde{\Phi}
, many of them were constructed as ties of the train track neighborhood. We will later see that analyzing the images of these tie edges under the 1–cocycle
c_{V}
will enable us to determine the images under
c_{V}
of all the edges of the triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
that are contained in
\widetilde{\Phi}
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
\widetilde{k}
be an oriented tie of the train track neighborhood
\widetilde{\Phi}
that is generic, in the sense that it is not contained in a switch tie, and let
\widetilde{T}_{-}
and
\widetilde{T}_{+}
be the components of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that respectively contain the negative and positive endpoints of
\widetilde{k}
. For every component
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
that separates
\widetilde{T}_{-}
from
\widetilde{T}_{+}
, counterclockwise label its vertices as
x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
in such a way that the side
x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}
faces
\widetilde{T}_{-}
. Consider the flags
E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})
,
