F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})
,
G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for each such component
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
, and use the same labelling conventions for
\widetilde{T}_{+}
. Then,

\displaystyle c_{V}(\widetilde{k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

where the sum is over all components
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
separating
\widetilde{T}_{-}
from
\widetilde{T}_{+}
and where, as in Proposition
30
,

\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{(a+c)b}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
33
in a special case.

We again consider a very special case, where
\widetilde{T}_{-}
is the base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
, and where
\widetilde{T}_{+}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

A key property of generic ties is that the components of
\widetilde{k}-\widetilde{\lambda}
containing each of the endpoints of
\widetilde{k}
are disjoint from the barriers. (Note that this fails at each switch for the boundary tie of the left incoming branch.)

In particular, for the negative endpoint
\widetilde{u}_{-}
of
\widetilde{k}
, the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{u}_{-})
is determined by the properties that the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
is its ascending flag, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
is its descending flag, and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
is spanned by the sum of the basis elements. By our normalization of the Hitchin representations
\rho_{t}
, these two flags and this line are independent of
t
. It follows that

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{u}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{u}_{-})^{-1}_{|t=0}=0.

For the positive endpoint
\widetilde{u}_{+}
of
\widetilde{k}
, the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{u}_{+})
is determined by the properties that the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})
is its ascending flag, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})
is its descending flag, and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})^{(1)}
is spanned by the sum of the basis elements.

To compute the variation of
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{u}_{+})
, we use the formula

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(w)_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w)+\dot{B}(\widetilde{T}_{+})\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(w)

of Proposition
30
for each
w\in\{x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}},y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}},z_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}\}
, where
\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
is as repeated in the statement of Lemma
33
, and where

\dot{B}(\widetilde{T}_{+})=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }w=x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}\\
0&\text{if }w=y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}+\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{+},x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}^{abc}&\text{if }w=z_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}.\end{cases}

Note that
\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})=0
and
\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})=0
since the shearing map
S^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}(t)\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
fixes the flags
E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})
and
F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})
. Also, the left eruption
L_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}^{abc}(t)
respects the line
G_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}^{(1)}
, and therefore
\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}G_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}^{abc}G_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}^{(1)}=0
. It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\dot{C}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})\in T_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\dot{C}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})\in T_{F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})^{(1)}{}_{|t=0}

\displaystyle=\dot{C}\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})^{(1)}\in T_{G_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}^{(1)}}\mathbb{RP}^{n-1}

for the same element

\dot{C}=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{0}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}).

Since the projective basis
\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})
is determined by the flags
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})
,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})
and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{+}})^{(1)}
, it follows that

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})_{|t=0}=\dot{C}\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+}).

Therefore,

c_{V}(\widetilde{k})=\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{+})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{+})^{-1}_{|t=0}-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{B}_{t}(\widetilde{v}_{-})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\widetilde{v}_{-})^{-1}_{|t=0}=\dot{C},

which is exactly what we were supposed to prove in this special case where
\widetilde{T}_{-}
is the base component
\widetilde{T}_{0}
and
\widetilde{T}_{+}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}}
of
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
33
in the general case.

The general case can be deduced from the special case by the same argument that we used for Lemma
32
.

Namely consider the projective map
A_{t}\in\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
sending the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})
, the flag
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})
to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})
, and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{0}})^{(1)}
to
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})^{(1)}
. If the Hitchin representation
\rho_{t}^{\prime}
is obtained by conjugating
\rho_{t}
with
A_{t}
, the flags
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})
,
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(y_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})
and the line
\mathcal{F}_{\rho_{t}}(z_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})^{(1)}
are now independent of
t
, and we can apply the special case to
\rho_{t}^{\prime}
. As in the proof of Lemma
32
, the formula for
\rho_{t}
is then obtained from the formula for
\rho_{t}^{\prime}
by a simple conjugation by
A_{0}
.
∎

5.4.
Opening zippers

An obvious problem with the formula of Lemma
33
is that it involves an infinite sum, which would be cumbersome to directly use in cup-product computations. So, as in
[
SB01
,
Zey21
]
, we will use an approximation based on the idea of opening zippers in a train track neighborhood to make it arbitrarily close to the geodesic lamination
\lambda
.

The operation of zipper opening replaces a train track neighborhood
\Phi
of the geodesic lamination
\lambda
by one that more closely approximates
\lambda
. More precisely, let
a
be an arc in
\Phi
that starts at a switch point of
\Phi
, is disjoint from
\lambda
and transverse to the ties of
\Phi
, and ends on a tie that is not a switch tie. Splitting
\Phi
along
a
gives a new train track neighborhood
\Phi^{\prime}
of
\lambda
. We say that
\Phi^{\prime}
is obtained from
\Phi
by
zipper opening along
a
. See Figure
11
, and
[
PH92
, §1.7, §2.4]
for details.

Instead of a single arc
a
, we can also split
\Phi
along a family of (necessarily disjoint) such arcs issued from distinct switch points of
\Phi
. In particular, for a given integer
\delta\geqslant 1
we can take, for each switch point
s
, an arc
a_{s}
as above that is issued from
s
and crosses exactly
\delta
switch ties. If the train track neighborhood
\Phi_{\delta}
is obtained from
\Phi
by zipper opening along the union of these arcs
a_{s}
, we say that
\Phi_{\delta}
is obtained from
\Phi
by
zipper opening up to depth
\delta
. One easily sees that, up to isotopy,
\Phi_{\delta}
depends only on
\Phi
and
\delta
.

a

\Phi

\Phi^{\prime}

\lambda

\lambda

Figure 11.
Opening a zipper

Lemma 34
.

Let
\Phi_{0}
be a train track neighborhood of the geodesic lamination
\lambda
, let
\Phi_{\delta}
be obtained from
\Phi_{0}
by a zipper opening up to depth
\delta
, and let
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\delta}
be the preimage of
\Phi_{\delta}
in the universal cover
\widetilde{S}
. Then, for every tie
\widetilde{k}
of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\delta}
and with the conventions of Lemma
33
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

c_{V}(\widetilde{k})=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})

where
C
and the other constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O(\ )
depend only on the Hitchin representation
\rho
, the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
, the original train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
, and a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
containing the tie
\widetilde{k}
.

Proof.

By construction, the tie
\widetilde{k}
of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\delta}
is contained in a tie
\widetilde{k}_{0}
of the original train track
\Phi_{0}
.

In addition to our earlier estimates, the argument is based on a classical measure of the combinatorial complexity of a component of
\widetilde{k}_{0}-\widetilde{\lambda}
with respect to
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
. Such a component is of the form
\widetilde{k}_{0}\cap\widetilde{T}
for some component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
. If none of the endpoints
\widetilde{k}_{0}\cap\widetilde{T}
is an endpoint of
\widetilde{k}_{0}
, the two sides of
\widetilde{T}
passing through these endpoints are asymptotic to each other on one side of
\widetilde{k}_{0}
, and on the other side follow a common path of
r(\widetilde{T})\geqslant 1
branches of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
before diverging at some switch of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
. This number
r(\widetilde{T})
is the
combinatorial depth
of
\widetilde{k}_{0}\cap\widetilde{T}
in the train track neighborhood
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
. By convention,
r(\widetilde{T})=0
if
\widetilde{k}_{0}\cap\widetilde{T}
contains one of the endpoints of
\widetilde{k}_{0}
.

This combinatorial depth actually played a crucial role in the proof of the estimates of Lemmas
26
and
27
that we borrowed from
[
BD17
]
. One key ingredient was the following:

Sublemma 35
.

There exist constants
C^{\prime}
,
C^{\prime\prime}
,
D^{\prime}
,
D^{\prime\prime}>0
, depending only on the negatively curved metric
m_{0}
that we used to define geodesic laminations and on the train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
, such that

D^{\prime}\mathrm{e}^{-C^{\prime}r(\widetilde{T})}\leqslant\ell(\widetilde{k}_{0}\cap\widetilde{T})\leqslant D^{\prime\prime}\mathrm{e}^{-C^{\prime\prime}r(\widetilde{T})}

for every component
\widetilde{k}_{0}\cap\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{k}_{0}\cap\widetilde{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Before they diverge at some switch of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
, the two sides of
\widetilde{T}
travel together over a length that is bounded above and below by a constant times
r(\widetilde{T})
. The estimate then follows from an estimate using the negative curvature of
m_{0}
. The constants
C^{\prime}
,
C^{\prime\prime}
,
D^{\prime}
,
D^{\prime\prime}>0
depend on curvature bounds for
m_{0}
, on the “lengths” of the branches of
\Phi_{0}
, and on a lower bound on the angles between the leaves of
\lambda
and the ties of
\Phi_{0}
.
∎

We are now ready to begin the proof of Lemma
34
. From the formula

\displaystyle c_{V}(\widetilde{k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}+\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})

of Lemma
33
, we need to show that

\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

for some constant
C>0
.

For this, we will use the estimates that already appeared in our proofs of Propositions
23
and
30
. However, the quantity

\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{(a+c)b}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime},\end{cases}

appearing in Lemma
33
is not exactly identical to the similar quantity occurring in Proposition
30
, which we rewrite here as

\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\left(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}^{abc}+\dot{S}^{(a+c)b}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}.\end{cases}

The difference is that the base triangle
\widetilde{T}_{0}
is replaced by the component
\widetilde{T}_{-}
, which depends on the tie
\widetilde{k}
of the train track neighborhood
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\delta}
. Some additional care is therefore needed to guarantee uniformity for the estimates.

For this, we first consider the case where
\widetilde{T}_{-}
separates
\widetilde{T}_{+}
from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. We then use the quasi-additivity property of Proposition
19
which, after taking derivatives, tells us that

\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{-})+\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})-\sum_{b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n-a}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{ab^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{-},x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{-}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{-}\\
\displaystyle\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{-})+\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})-\sum_{a^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n-b}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{bc^{\prime}a^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T}_{-},y_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ faces the side }y_{\widetilde{T}_{-}}z_{\widetilde{T}_{-}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}_{-}.\end{cases}

This enables us to write the term
\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
of Lemma
33
as

\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})+\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})

where
\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
is as above and

\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{-})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{-},x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})\left(\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\\
\displaystyle-\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{-})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{-},x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})\left(\dot{S}^{(a+c)b}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{(a+c)b}_{G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\widetilde{T}_{+}\text{ faces the side }y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}\text{ of }\widetilde{T}^{\prime}.\end{cases}

This will enable us to split the analysis into two steps.

Sublemma 36
.

For
\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
as above, there exists
C>0
such that

\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}),

where the constant
C>0
and the constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O(\ )
depend only on a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
containing
\widetilde{k}
, on the Hitchin representation
\rho
, and on the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
.

Proof.

This is a simple consequence of the estimates that we used in the proofs of Propositions
23
and
30
.

Indeed, in the proof of Proposition
30
,
\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
(called
\dot{A}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
in that statement) occurred as the derivative at
t=0
of a quantity
A_{t}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
defined in that proof, which makes sense as a holomorphic function of
t\in\mathbb{C}
with
|t|<1
. Lemma
28
asserts that
A_{t}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+O\left(\ell(\widetilde{k}\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})^{\nu}\right)
for some
\nu
. An application of Pick’s lemma then shows that

\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}O\left(\ell(\widetilde{k}\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})^{\nu}\right).

Sublemma
35
shows that
\ell(\widetilde{k}\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=O(\mathrm{e}^{-C^{\prime\prime}r(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})})
for some constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
and, by definition of zipper openings,
r(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})\geqslant\delta
for each component
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
occurring in the above sum. It then follows from the second part of Lemma
27
that

\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}O\left(\ell(\widetilde{k}\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})^{\nu}\right)=O(\mathrm{e}^{-\nu^{\prime}C^{\prime\prime}\delta})=O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

for any
\nu^{\prime}<\nu
and the constant
C=\nu^{\prime}C^{\prime\prime}
. This concludes the proof of Sublemma
36
.
∎

Sublemma 37
.

For
\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
as above, there exists
C>0
such that

\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}),

where the exponent
\nu
and the constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O(\ )
depend only on a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
containing
\widetilde{k}
, on the Hitchin representation
\rho
, and on the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
.

Proof.

In order to bound
\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
, let us first focus on the case where
\widetilde{T}_{+}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}
of
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
. The other case will be identical.

In this case,

\displaystyle\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{-})\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{-},x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})\left(\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{a(b+c)}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}\right).

Because the flag map
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}\colon\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}\to\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is Hölder continuous
[
Lab06
, Thm. 4.1]
,

\displaystyle\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}-\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=O\Big(d\big((E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}},F_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}),(E_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}},G_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=O\big(d(x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}},x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}})^{\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=O\big(\ell(\widetilde{k}\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})^{\nu}\big)

for some
\nu>0
, where we use the same symbol
d(\ ,\ )
to denote the distance in the space of flag pairs for the first line, and the distance in the space of geodesics of
\widetilde{S}
for the second line.

The coefficients
\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T}_{-},x_{\widetilde{T}_{-}})
are obviously uniformly bounded, since they take only finitely many values.

To bound the coefficients
\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}^{\prime})
, a combinatorial argument using the Switch Relation of §
1.3
(compare
[
Bon97
, Lem. 6]
) shows that

\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{-})=O\big(r(\widetilde{T}_{-})\big)

with the constant depending only on the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
. Also,
r(T_{-})\leqslant\delta
by definition of zipper openings. Therefore,

\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{0},\widetilde{T}_{-})=O(\delta).

Combining all these estimates gives us that

\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=O\left(\delta\ell(\widetilde{k}\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})^{\nu}\right),

in the case where
\widetilde{T}_{+}
faces the side
x_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}
of
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
.

The same argument gives us an identical estimate
in the other case, where
\widetilde{T}_{+}
faces the side
y_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}z_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}}
of
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}
.

Now, by another application of Sublemma
35
and Lemma
27
,

\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})=\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}O\big(\delta\ell(\widetilde{k}\cap\widetilde{T}^{\prime})^{\nu}\big)=O(\delta\mathrm{e}^{-\nu^{\prime}C^{\prime\prime}\delta})=O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

for any
\nu^{\prime}<\nu
and
C<\nu^{\prime}C^{\prime\prime}
. This concludes the proof of Sublemma
37
.
∎

By taking the smallest of the two, we can arrange that the constants
C
of Sublemmas
36
and
37
coincide. These statements then prove that

\displaystyle c_{V}(\widetilde{k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}+\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}^{\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})+\sum_{\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\text{ between }\widetilde{T}_{-}\text{ and }\widetilde{T}_{+}}\dot{A}^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{T}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

and completes the proof of Lemma
34
in the special case considered, where
\widetilde{T}_{-}
separates
\widetilde{T}_{0}
from
\widetilde{T}_{+}
.

We will deduce the general case from this special case, but we need some care to guarantee the uniformity of the estimates. The statement of Lemma
34
involves a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
containing the tie
\widetilde{k}
. This compact subset
K
meets only finitely branches of the original train track neighborhood
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
.

For each oriented branch
e
of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
(namely a branch of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
with an orientation of the corresponding branch of the train track
\widetilde{\Psi}_{0}
) meeting
K
, the orientation of
e
determines an orientation for the leaves of
\widetilde{\lambda}
that pass through
e
, and these orientations are all parallel. Among these leaves, let
g_{e}^{\mathrm{left}}
be the one that is most to the left. Since the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(S)
acts minimally on the space of geodesics of
\widetilde{S}
, these exists
\gamma_{e}\in\pi_{1}(S)
such that
\gamma_{e}g_{e}^{\mathrm{left}}
is oriented to the left as seen from the base triangle
\widetilde{T}_{0}
. This guarantees that the images under
\gamma_{e}
of all the leaves of
\widetilde{\lambda}
passing through
e
are in the same component of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{T}_{0}
and are oriented to the left as seen from
\widetilde{T}_{0}
.

If
\widetilde{k}
is an oriented tie of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\delta}
contained in
K
, let
e
be the branch of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
that contains it. Orient
e
so that the orientation that it induces on the leaves of
\widetilde{\lambda}
passing through
\widetilde{k}
points to the left of the orientation of
\widetilde{k}
. Then,
\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{-}
separates
\widetilde{T}_{0}
from
\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{+}
, and we can therefore apply the special case to the tie
\gamma_{e}\widetilde{k}
. This gives

c_{V}(\gamma_{e}\widetilde{k})=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{-},\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{+}}}+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}),

where the exponent
\nu
and the constant in
O(\ )
depend only on the union on the finitely many compact subsets
\gamma_{e^{\prime}}K
as
e^{\prime}
ranges over all branches of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
meeting
K
(as well as on the Hitchin representation
\rho
and the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
). Then

\displaystyle c_{V}(\widetilde{k})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho(\gamma_{e}^{-1})\big(c_{V}(\gamma_{e}\widetilde{k})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{-},\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{+})\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho(\gamma_{e}^{-1})(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\gamma_{e}\widetilde{T}_{+}}})+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V}^{ab}(\widetilde{T}_{-},\widetilde{T}_{+})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}F_{\widetilde{T}_{+}}}+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}),

where the constant in
O(\ )
now includes, in addition, the distorsion in
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
coming from the finitely many isomorphisms
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho(\gamma_{e^{\prime}}^{-1})
that can occur. For the last equality, use the
\pi_{1}(S)
–equivariance property of Lemma
19
.

This completes the proof of Lemma
34
.
∎

6.
Computing and estimating the cup-product

In §
2.1
, we saw that the Atiyah-Bott-Goldman symplectic form is defined in terms of the evaluation on the fundamental class
[S]\in H_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z})
of the cup-product
[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}]\in H^{2}(S;\mathbb{R})
of the Weil classes
[c_{V_{1}}]
,
[c_{V_{2}}]\in H^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
associated to two tangent vectors
V_{1}
,
V_{2}\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
. Now that we have an explicit description of the cocycles
c_{V_{1}}
,
c_{V_{2}}\in C^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R}))
, we will use the triangulation of §
5.2
to compute, or at least estimate in a first step, this cup-product.

6.1.
A remark on the computation of cup-products

We begin with a general observation on cup-products in dimension 2. This property was used without discussion in
[
SB01
,
Zey21
]
, but does not seem to be well-known and therefore may deserve some explanation.

Let
\Delta_{1}=\{(t_{0},t_{1})\in\mathbb{R}^{2};t_{0}+t_{1}=1\}
and
\Delta_{2}=\{(t_{0},t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{3};t_{0}+t_{1}+t_{2}=1\}
denote the standard 1– and 2–simplex. A singular 2–simplex
f\colon\Delta_{2}\to S
has three sides: the
front side
is the 1–simplex
F^{\mathrm{front}}f\colon\Delta_{1}\to S
defined by
F^{\mathrm{front}}f(t_{0},t_{1})=(t_{0},t_{1},0)
, the
middle side

F^{\mathrm{mid}}f
is defined by
F^{\mathrm{mid}}f(t_{0},t_{1})=(t_{0},0,t_{1})
, and the
back side

F^{\mathrm{back}}f
is defined by
F^{\mathrm{back}}f(t_{0},t_{1})=(0,t_{0},t_{1})
. Then the boundary
\partial f
is the 1–chain
F^{\mathrm{back}}f-F^{\mathrm{mid}}f+F^{\mathrm{front}}f
.

The currently popular definition (see for instance
[
Hat02
, §3.2]
) of the cup-product
[c_{1}]{\smallsmile}[c_{2}]\in H^{2}(S;\mathbb{R})
of two homology classes
[c_{1}]
,
[c_{2}]\in H^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
is the class of the 2–cochain
c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}
defined by

c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}(f)=K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)\in\mathbb{R}

where
K\colon\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})\times\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
is the Killing form and where
\widetilde{f}
is an arbitrary lift of
f
to the universal cover
\widetilde{S}
. The invariance of the Killing form under the adjoint representation guarantees that this does not depend on the choice of the lift
\widetilde{f}
. This definition has the advantage of simplicity, but the antisymmetry of the cup-product then becomes a nontrivial result (see for instance
[
Hat02
, Th. 3.14]
).

We now express the fundamental class
[S]\in H_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z})
by a 2–chain based on a triangulation
\Sigma
of the oriented surface
S
. For each face
f
of this triangulation, choose an arbitrary parametrization of this face as a singular 2–simplex
f\colon\Delta_{2}\to S
, in such a way that the orientation of the front and back sides
F^{\mathrm{front}}f
,
F^{\mathrm{back}}f
coincide with the boundary orientation of
f
(while the orientation of the middle side
F^{\mathrm{mid}}f
is the opposite of this boundary orientation); we are here orienting the 1–simplex
\Delta_{1}
from
(1,0)
to
(0,1)
. Let the 2–chain

\gamma=\sum_{f}f\in C_{2}(S)

be the sum of the simplices
f
thus associated to the faces of the triangulation
\Sigma
. However, this chain is in general not closed.

An edge
k
of the triangulation
\Sigma
separates two faces
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
of
\Sigma
, and therefore is the image of a side
F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
and a side
F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
with
i_{1}(k)
,
i_{2}(k)\in\{\mathrm{front},\mathrm{back},\mathrm{mid}\}
. If
F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
and
F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
induce the same orientation on
k
, we can arrange that
F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}=F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
as maps
\Delta_{1}\to S
. Note that this happens precisely when
i_{1}(k)
,
i_{2}(k)
are not in the same subset
\{\mathrm{front},\mathrm{back}\}
or
\{\mathrm{mid}\}
. Consequently, the two sides
F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
and
F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
occur with opposite signs in the expressions of
\partial f_{1}
and
\partial f_{2}
, and they cancel out in the expression of
\partial\gamma
. In other words, we can arrange that the contribution of the edge
k
to
\partial\gamma
is equal to 0, in this case where
F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
and
F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
induce the same orientation on
k
.

When
F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
and
F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
induce opposite orientations on
k
, there exists a singular 2-simplex
f_{k}\colon\Delta_{2}\to S
, with image contained in
k
, whose front side
F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{k}
coincides with
F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
, whose back side
F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{k}
coincides with
F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
, and whose middle side
F^{\mathrm{mid}}f_{k}
is constant, valued at the initial vertex of the front side
F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{k}=F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
(which is also the terminal vertex of the back side
F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{k}=F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
). Also, let
f_{k}^{\prime}\colon\Delta_{2}\to S
be constant, valued at the same initial vertex of
F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{k}
. Then,

\displaystyle\partial(f_{k}+f_{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{k}-F^{\mathrm{mid}}f_{k}+F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{k}+F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{k}^{\prime}-F^{\mathrm{mid}}f_{k}^{\prime}+F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}+F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}

since
F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{k}=F^{i_{2}(k)}f_{2}
,
F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{k}=F^{i_{1}(k)}f_{1}
, and
F^{\mathrm{mid}}f_{k}
,
F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{k}^{\prime}
,
F^{\mathrm{mid}}f_{k}^{\prime}
,
F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{k}^{\prime}
are all equal to the same constant map.

To unify the two cases, let
\gamma_{k}\in C_{2}(S)
be defined by

\gamma_{k}=\begin{cases}-f_{k}-f_{k}^{\prime}&\text{ if }i_{1}(k),i_{2}(k)\in\{\mathrm{front},\mathrm{back}\}\\
f_{k}+f_{k}^{\prime}&\text{ if }i_{1}(k)=i_{2}(k)\in\{\mathrm{mid}\}\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

We have done everything so that the 2–chain

\gamma_{S}=\sum_{f}f+\sum_{k}\gamma_{k}\in C_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z})

is now closed, where the sums are over all faces and over all edges of the triangulation
\Sigma
, respectively. A degree argument applied to a point in the interior of a face shows that the homology class
[\gamma_{S}]\in H_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z})
is equal to the fundamental class
[S]
.

Lemma 38
.

For any two cocycles
c_{1}
,
c_{2}\in C^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
, the evaluation of the cup-product
[c_{1}]{\smallsmile}[c_{2}]\in H^{2}(S;\mathbb{R})
over the fundamental class
[S]\in H_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z})
is equal to

\langle[c_{1}]{\smallsmile}[c_{2}],[S]\rangle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{f}\Big(K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)-K\big(c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)\Big),

where the sum ranges over all faces
f
of the triangulation
\Sigma
considered as
2
–simplices whose parametrization is compatible with the orientation of
S
.

Proof.

Let us represent
[S]
by the cycle
\gamma_{S}
as above. Then

c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}(\gamma_{S})=\sum_{f}K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)+\sum_{k}c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}),

where
\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}\in C_{2}(\widetilde{S})
is the lift of
\gamma_{k}
contained in an arbitrary lift
\widetilde{k}
of the edge
k
.

To make the formula more symmetric, we now take advantage of the nontrivial property (see for instance
[
Hat02
, Th. 3.14]
) that the cup-product is antisymmetric at the homological level (although not at the chain level). Namely,
[c_{2}]{\smallsmile}[c_{1}]=-[c_{1}]{\smallsmile}[c_{2}]
in
H^{2}(S;\mathbb{R})
. This enables us to rewrite the formula as

\displaystyle\langle[c_{1}]{\smallsmile}[c_{2}],[S]\rangle

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\langle[c_{1}]{\smallsmile}[c_{2}],[S]\rangle-\langle[c_{2}]{\smallsmile}[c_{1}],[S]\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{f}\Big(K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)-K\big(c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{k}\left(c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}-c_{2}{\smallsmile}c_{1}\right)(\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}).

We claim that
\left(c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}-c_{2}{\smallsmile}c_{1}\right)(\widetilde{\gamma}_{k})=0
for every edge
k
. This is certainly clear when
\gamma_{k}=0
. Otherwise,
\gamma_{k}=\pm(f_{k}+f_{k}^{\prime})
.

Since
c(\kappa)=0
for any constant 1–simplex
\kappa
and any 1–cocycle
c
(as a chain,
\kappa
is the boundary of a constant 2–simplex), the evaluations of
c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}
and
c_{2}{\smallsmile}c_{1}
on the constant simplex
\widetilde{f}_{k}^{\prime}
are both equal to
0
.

Since
\partial\widetilde{f}_{k}=F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{k}-F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{k}+F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k}
, we have that

c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{k})=-c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k})+c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{k})=-c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k})

for each
i=1
,
2
, since
c_{i}
is closed and the middle side
F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{k}
is constant. It follows that

\displaystyle\left(c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}-c_{2}{\smallsmile}c_{1}\right)(\widetilde{f}_{k})

\displaystyle=K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{k})\big)-K\big(c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k}),c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{k})\big)

\displaystyle=-K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k})\big)+K\big(c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k}),c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{k})\big)=0.

This proves that
\left(c_{1}{\smallsmile}c_{2}-c_{2}{\smallsmile}c_{1}\right)(\widetilde{\gamma}_{k})=0
for every edge
k
, and completes the computation.
∎

The formula of Lemma
38
will enable us to take advantage of the following symmetry.

Lemma 39
.

The formula of Lemma
38
is independent of the parametrization of each face
f
of the triangulation
\Sigma
as a singular
2
–simplex
f\colon\Delta_{2}\to S
(as long as this parametrization is compatible with the orientation of
S
, in the sense that the orientation of the front and back sides coincides with the boundary orientation).

Proof.

We need to show invariance if we “rotate” such a parametrization
f\colon\Delta_{2}\to S
to a new parametrization
f^{\prime}\colon\Delta_{2}\to S
such that the front side
F^{\mathrm{front}}f^{\prime}
is equal to the back side
F^{\mathrm{back}}f
, the middle side
F^{\mathrm{mid}}f^{\prime}
is the front side
F^{\mathrm{front}}f
with the orientation reversed, and the back side
F^{\mathrm{back}}f^{\prime}
is the middle side
F^{\mathrm{mid}}f
with the orientation reversed. Then

\displaystyle c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{front}}f^{\prime})

\displaystyle=c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{back}}f)

\displaystyle c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{back}}f^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}f)=-c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{front}}f)-c_{i}(F^{\mathrm{back}}f)

for each
i=1
,
2
. Expanding the contribution

K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}^{\prime}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}^{\prime})\big)-K\big(c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}^{\prime}),c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}^{\prime})\big)

of this new parametrization
f^{\prime}
and using the bilinearity and symmetry of the Killing form
K
, we readily see that it is equal to the contribution

K\big(c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)-K\big(c_{2}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{1}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)

of the old parametrization
f
.
∎

6.2.
General setup

We now return to our computation of the evaluation

\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle\in\mathbb{R}

of the cup-product
[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}]\in H^{2}(S;\mathbb{R})
on the fundamental class
[S]\in H_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z})
, for the Weil classes
[c_{V_{1}}]
,
[c_{V_{2}}]\in H^{1}(S;\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\rho})
corresponding to tangent vectors
V_{1}
,
V_{2}\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
. For this we will use the description of the classes
[c_{V_{1}}]
,
[c_{V_{2}}]
given in §
5.1
and §
5.3
, as well as the triangulation
\Sigma
introduced in §
5.2
.

A consequence of Lemma
39
is that, in the formula for
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
given by Lemma
38
, the contribution of most faces of the triangulation
\Sigma
is equal to 0.

Lemma 40
.

Suppose that at least one side of the face
f
of the triangulation
\Sigma
is disjoint from the geodesic lamination
\lambda
and from the barriers
\beta_{T}
. Then the contribution of
f
to the formula for
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
given by Lemma
38
is equal to
0
.

Proof.

Using the flexibility provided by Lemma
39
, we can choose the parametrization of
f
so that its front face
F^{\mathrm{front}}f
is disjoint from
\lambda
and from the barriers
\beta_{T}
. Then, Lemma
31
shows that

c_{V_{1}}(\widetilde{F}^{\mathrm{front}}f)=c_{V_{2}}(\widetilde{F}^{\mathrm{front}}f)=0
, and it follows that the contribution of
f
to the formula of Lemma
38
is equal to 0.
∎

In the triangulation
\Sigma
of §
5.2
, there are only a few exceptional faces whose three sides all meet the union of the geodesic lamination
\lambda
and the barriers
\beta_{T}
, and may therefore have a nontrivial contribution to
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
.

The first type is that of the face
f
that contains the central point
\pi_{T}
of a barrier
\beta_{T}
. By our assumption that each edge of
\Sigma
that is outside of the train track neighborhood meets the barriers in at most one point, each side of
f
meets the barriers in exactly one point.

The second type occurs near the switches of
\Phi
. More precisely, near each switch, there are two faces of
\Sigma
whose sides all meet the union of
\lambda
and of the barriers
\beta_{T}
. See Figure
13
below.

By inspection of the construction of the triangulation
\Sigma
, these triangles, one for each component of
\Phi-S
and two for each switch, are the only ones whose contribution to
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
may be nonzero. We will now compute the contribution of these faces.

6.3.
The contribution of the complement of the train track

We consider the first type of faces of
\Sigma
that may have a nontrivial contribution to
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
, namely a face
f
that contains the center
\pi_{T}
of a barrier
\beta_{T}
. By construction of the triangulation
\Sigma
in §
5.2
, there is exactly one such face in each component of the complement
S-\Phi
of the train track neighborhood
\Phi
.

Consider the component
T
of
S-\lambda
containing this face
f
. For the train track
\Psi
associated to the train track neighborhood
\Phi
, let
U
be the component of
S-\Psi
associated to
T
by the correspondence of Proposition
6
. Arbitrarily pick a boundary corner
s_{U}
of
U
, corresponding to a switch of
\Psi
.

Lift
T
to a component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
, and
f
to a triangle
\widetilde{f}
contained in
\widetilde{T}
. Also, let
x_{\widetilde{T}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
be the vertex of
\widetilde{T}
associated to the corner
s_{U}
by the correspondence of Proposition
6
, and index the other two vertices as
y_{\widetilde{T}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}}
in such a way that
x_{\widetilde{T}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}}
,
z_{\widetilde{T}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
occur counterclockwise in this order around
\widetilde{T}
. As usual, let
E_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(x_{\widetilde{T}})
,
F_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(y_{\widetilde{T}})
,
G_{\widetilde{T}}=\mathcal{F}_{\rho}(z_{\widetilde{T}})\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the flags associated to these vertices by the flag map
\mathcal{F}_{\rho}
.

Finally, let
\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\in\mathbb{R}
denote the directional derivative of the triangle invariant
\tau_{\rho}^{abc}(U,s_{U})
in the direction of the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
. Namely,

\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(U,s_{U})=\frac{d}{dt}\tau^{abc}_{\rho_{t}}(U,s_{U})_{|t=0}

for every curve
t\mapsto[\rho_{t}]
with
\rho_{0}=\rho
and
\frac{d}{dt}[\rho_{t}]_{|t=0}=V
.

\widetilde{f}

F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}

F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}

F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}

x_{\widetilde{T}}

y_{\widetilde{T}}

z_{\widetilde{T}}

\beta_{\widetilde{T}}

Figure 12.
A face
\widetilde{f}
containing the center
\pi_{\widetilde{T}}
of a barrier
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}

Lemma 41
.

The contribution of the face
f
to the formula for
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
given by Lemma
38
is equal to

\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\left(\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})-\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})\right)K\bigl(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc},\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr),

where
K\colon\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})\times\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
is the Killing form, and where
\dot{L}_{EFG}^{abc}
and
\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc}
are the infinitesimal left and right eruptions of §
4.4
.

Proof.

The barrier
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
divides
\widetilde{T}
into three triangles, each adjacent to one side of
\widetilde{T}
. With the flexibility provided by Lemma
39
, choose the parametrization of the face
f
so that the front side
F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}
goes from the component of
\widetilde{T}-\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
that is adjacent to
x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}}
to the component adjacent to
y_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
. Then the back side
F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}
goes from the component adjacent to
y_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
to the component adjacent to
x_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
, and the middle side
F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}
goes from the component adjacent to
x_{\widetilde{T}}y_{\widetilde{T}}
to the component adjacent to
x_{\widetilde{T}}z_{\widetilde{T}}
.

The contribution of
f
to
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
is then equal to

\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(K\big(c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)-K\big(c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})\big)\Big),

which is also equal to

\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(K\big(c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f})\big)-K\big(c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}),c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f})\big)\Big),

since
c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f})=c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f})+c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f})
as each
c_{V_{i}}
is closed.

Lemma
32
then provides

\displaystyle c_{V}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}

\displaystyle c_{V}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n}\dot{\tau}_{V}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

for each
V=V_{1}
or
V_{2}
. Since
\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(\widetilde{T},x_{\widetilde{T}})=\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(U,s_{U})
by the correspondence of Lemma
18
, the result immediately follows.
∎

We now compute the coefficient
K\bigl(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc},\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)
.

Lemma 42
.

For any maximum-span flag triple
(E,F,G)\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{3}
,

K\bigl(\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc},\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)=\begin{cases}2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\text{ or }b\leqslant b^{\prime}\\
2ab^{\prime}-2bc^{\prime}+2b^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
2ab^{\prime}+2ac^{\prime}-2a^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Note the remarkable, and unexpected, property that
K\bigl(\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc},\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)
is independent of the maximum-span flag triple
\bigl(E,F,G\bigr)
(whereas Proposition
11
shows that the moduli space of these triples has dimension
\frac{1}{2}(n-1)(n-2)
).

Proof.

This is essentially
[
SZ17
, Lem. 6.7]
, which is here restated with different notation and conventions.

By definition,

\displaystyle\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-b^{\prime}-c^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a^{\prime})}}\oplus\tfrac{a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b^{\prime})}}\oplus\tfrac{a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a^{\prime})}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b^{\prime})}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c^{\prime})}}=\tfrac{a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\tfrac{a+c}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\tfrac{-b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}

\displaystyle=0\,\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}-\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}=P_{FGE}^{bca}-\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}

for the two projection maps

\displaystyle P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a^{\prime})}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b^{\prime})}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle P_{FGE}^{bca}

\displaystyle=0\,\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle K\bigl(\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc},\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc}\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(P_{FGE}^{bca}-\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\right)\left(\tfrac{a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=-2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+2a^{\prime}\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}+2b\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}-2\tfrac{a^{\prime}b}{n}\mathrm{Tr}\,\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}

\displaystyle=-2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+2a^{\prime}b+2ba^{\prime}-2\tfrac{a^{\prime}b}{n}n

\displaystyle=-2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+2a^{\prime}b.

To compute
\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
, we distinguish cases. Note that these cases will overlap.

Case 1.

a\geqslant a^{\prime}
.

The image of the projection
P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
is equal to
E^{(a^{\prime})}\subset E^{(a)}
, and is therefore contained in the kernel
E^{(a)}\oplus G^{(c)}
of
P_{FGE}^{bca}
. Therefore,

\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}=\mathrm{Tr}\,0=0

and

K\bigl(\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc},\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)=-2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+2a^{\prime}b=2a^{\prime}b.

Case 2.

b\leqslant b^{\prime}
.

Similarly, the image
F^{(b)}
of
P_{FGE}^{bca}
is contained in the kernel
F^{(b^{\prime})}\oplus G^{(c^{\prime})}
of
P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
, which implies that

\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}=\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}=\mathrm{Tr}\,0=0

and

K\bigl(\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc},\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)=-2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+2a^{\prime}b=2a^{\prime}b.

Case 3.

a\leqslant a^{\prime}
,
b\geqslant b^{\prime}
and
c\geqslant c^{\prime}
.
Here we need to be more explicit. Let
\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}\}
be a basis for
\mathbb{R}^{n}
that is adapted to the flag
E
, in the sense that each subspace
E^{(a^{\prime\prime})}
is spanned by the first
a^{\prime\prime}
vectors
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
, …,
e_{a^{\prime\prime}}
. Similarly, let
\{f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{n}\}
be a basis adapted to
F
, and let
\{g_{1},g_{2},\dots,g_{n}\}
be a basis adapted to
G
. We will express the composition
P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}=\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{a},f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{b},g_{1},g_{2},\dots,g_{c}\}
for
\mathbb{R}^{n}=E^{(a)}\oplus F^{(b)}\oplus G^{(c)}
.

The map
P_{FGE}^{bca}
sends each
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
, …,
e_{a}
and each
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
, …,
g_{c}
to 0. Also, for
i\leqslant b^{\prime}\leqslant b
,

P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}(f_{i})=P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(f_{i})=0
. Therefore, in the matrix expressing
P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}
, the only columns that may be nonzero are those corresponding to the images of the basis elements
f_{b^{\prime}+1}
,
f_{b^{\prime}+2}
, …,
f_{b}
(with no such column when
b=b^{\prime}
).

Express these
f_{i}
, with
b^{\prime}<i\leqslant b
, in the basis
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}=\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{a^{\prime}},f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{b^{\prime}},g_{1},g_{2},\dots,g_{c^{\prime}}\}
. Namely,

f_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{a^{\prime}}\lambda_{ij}e_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{b^{\prime}}\mu_{ij}f_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{c^{\prime}}\nu_{ij}g_{j}

for some coefficients
\lambda_{ij}
,
\mu_{ij}
,
\nu_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then,

\displaystyle P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}(f_{i})

\displaystyle=P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(f_{i})=P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{a^{\prime}}\lambda_{ij}e_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{b^{\prime}}\mu_{ij}f_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{c^{\prime}}\nu_{ij}g_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{a^{\prime}}\lambda_{ij}e_{j}=f_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{b^{\prime}}\mu_{ij}f_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{c^{\prime}}\nu_{ij}g_{j}

which, because
i\leqslant b
,
b^{\prime}\leqslant b
and
c^{\prime}\leqslant c
, expresses
P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}(f_{i})
in the basis
\mathcal{B}
.

Therefore, in the matrix expressing
P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}
, the column corresponding to
f_{i}
with
b^{\prime}<i\leqslant b
has an entry
1
on the diagonal and possibly a few more nonzero entries above and below this diagonal. As a consequence, the diagonal entries of this matrix consist of
a+b^{\prime}
terms 0, followed by
b-b^{\prime}
terms 1, and then another
c
terms
0
.

This proves that

\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}=\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}P_{FGE}^{bca}=b-b^{\prime}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle K\bigl(\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc},\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=-2n\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+2a^{\prime}b

\displaystyle=-2n(b-b^{\prime})+2a^{\prime}b=2ab^{\prime}-2bc^{\prime}+2b^{\prime}c.

by using the properties that
a+b+c=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n
.

We now reach the last case.

Case 4.

a\leqslant a^{\prime}
,
b\geqslant b^{\prime}
and
c\leqslant c^{\prime}
.

The argument is very similar to the previous one. The difference is that we now express
P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}=\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{a^{\prime}},f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{b^{\prime}},g_{1},g_{2},\dots,g_{c^{\prime}}\}
for
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

The vectors
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
, …,
f_{b^{\prime}}
,
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
, …,
g_{c^{\prime}}
are sent to 0 by the projection
P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
. Also, for
i\leqslant a\leqslant a^{\prime}
,
P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(e_{i})=P_{FGE}^{bca}(e_{i})=0
. Therefore, in the matrix expressing
P_{EFG}^{abc}P_{FGE}^{b^{\prime}c^{\prime}a^{\prime}}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, the columns corresponding to
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
, …,
e_{a}
,
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
, …,
f_{b^{\prime}}
,
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
, …,
g_{c}
are all equal to 0.

Express the remaining vectors
e_{i}
, with
a<i\leqslant a^{\prime}
, in our earlier basis
\mathcal{B}
as

e_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{a}\lambda^{\prime}_{ij}e_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{b}\mu^{\prime}_{ij}f_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{c}\nu^{\prime}_{ij}g_{j}

for some coefficients
\lambda_{ij}^{\prime}
,
\mu_{ij}^{\prime}
,
\nu_{ij}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, as in the previous case, its image
P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(e_{i})
can be expressed in the basis
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(e_{i})

\displaystyle=P_{FGE}^{bca}(e_{i})=P_{FGE}^{bca}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{a}\lambda^{\prime}_{ij}e_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{b}\mu^{\prime}_{ij}f_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{c}\nu^{\prime}_{ij}g_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{b}\mu^{\prime}_{ij}f_{j}=e_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{a}\lambda^{\prime}_{ij}e_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{c}\nu^{\prime}_{ij}g_{j}.

Because
i>a
this shows that, in the matrix expressing
P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, the diagonal entry of the
i
–th column with
a<i\leqslant a^{\prime}
is equal to 1.

The conclusion is again that the diagonal entries of this matrix consist of
a
terms
0
, followed by
a^{\prime}-a
terms 1 and then
b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}
terms 0.
As a consequence,

\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}=a^{\prime}-a

and

\displaystyle K\bigl(\dot{R}_{EFG}^{abc},\dot{L}_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=-2n\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{bca}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}+2a^{\prime}b

\displaystyle=-2n(a^{\prime}-a)+2a^{\prime}b=2ab^{\prime}+2ac^{\prime}-2a^{\prime}c,

using again the properties that
a+b+c=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n
.
∎

We can now return to the contribution of the face
f
to the formula of Lemma
38
.

Lemma 43
.

Let
f
be the face of the triangulation
\Sigma
that contains the center
\pi_{T}
of the barrier
\beta_{T}
of
T
and, in the complement
S-\Psi
of the train track
\Psi
, let
U
be the component of
S-\Psi
corresponding to the component of
S-\Phi
that contains
f
. Then, for an arbitrary corner
s_{U}
of the triangle
U
, the contribution of
f
to the evaluation
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
of the cup product is equal to

\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\left(\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})-\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})\right)

where, as in Lemma
41
,
\dot{\tau}_{V}^{abc}(U,s_{U})
denotes the directional derivative of
\tau_{\rho}^{abc}(U,s_{U})
in the direction of the tangent vector
V\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
and where

C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}a^{\prime}b-ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
ac^{\prime}-a^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\\
b^{\prime}c-bc^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
b^{\prime}c-bc^{\prime}&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\\
ac^{\prime}-a^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
a^{\prime}b-ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Using the antisymmetry of the formula of Lemma
41
, it can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\left(\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})-\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left(K\bigl(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc},\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\bigr)-K\bigl(\dot{R}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}},\dot{L}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}^{abc}\bigr)\right).

The result then follows from a case-by-case application of Lemma
42
.
∎

Note the rotational symmetry
C^{bca}_{b^{\prime}c^{\prime}a^{\prime}}=C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
of the constants
C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
which shows that the output of Lemma
43
is, as expected, independent of the choice of the corner
s_{U}
for
U
.

6.4.
The contribution of the switches of the train track

Near a switch
s
of the train track
\Psi
or, more precisely, near the corresponding switch tie of the train track neighborhood
\Phi
, there are exactly two faces
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
of the triangulation
\Sigma
which may have a nontrivial contribution to the evaluation
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
, because their sides all meet the union of the geodesic lamination
\lambda
and of the barriers
\beta_{T}
(see Lemma
40
). These are illustrated in Figure
13
.

Let
k_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
and
k_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}
be two generic ties of the incoming branches
e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
and
e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}
, respectively, and orient these ties to the left as seen from the switch
s
. Also, let
k_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}}
be a small arc meeting in one point the branch of the barrier
\beta_{T}
that enters
\Phi
near
s
, and again orient this arc to the left as seen from
s
. See Figure
13
.
