The contribution of a switch

Lift the switch tie
s
to a switch tie
\widetilde{s}
of the train track neighborhood
\widetilde{\Phi}
in the universal cover
\widetilde{S}
. Then lift
k_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
,
k_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}
,
k_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}}
to oriented arcs
\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
,
\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}
,
\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}}
near
\widetilde{s}
.

Lemma 44
.

The contribution of the switch
s
to the evaluation
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
, namely the sum of the respective contributions of the faces
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
to the formula of Lemma
38
, is equal to

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigl(K\bigl(c_{V_{1}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}),c_{V_{2}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\bigr)-K\bigl(c_{V_{2}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}),c_{V_{1}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigl(K\bigl(c_{V_{1}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}),c_{V_{2}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}})\bigr)-K\bigl(c_{V_{2}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}),c_{V_{1}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}})\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad-\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigl(K\bigl(c_{V_{1}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}),c_{V_{2}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}})\bigr)-K\bigl(c_{V_{2}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}),c_{V_{1}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}})\bigr)\Bigr).

Proof.

Choose the parametrization of the faces
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
so that the sides
F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{1}
,
F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{1}
,
F^{\mathrm{front}}f_{2}
,
F^{\mathrm{back}}f_{2}
are as indicated in Figure
13
. Then, in the triangulation
\widetilde{\Sigma}
of the universal covering
\widetilde{S}
, lift
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
and their parametrizations to the corresponding two faces
\widetilde{f}_{1}
and
\widetilde{f}_{2}
that are near
\widetilde{s}
.

By construction, there is an arc
\widetilde{k}_{+}
that is disjoint from the union of the geodesic lamination
\widetilde{\lambda}
and the barriers
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
and goes from the positive endpoint of
F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1}
to the positive endpoint of
\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
; for instance, the arc can be chosen in the boundary of
\widetilde{\Phi}
. Similarly, pick an arc
\widetilde{k}_{-}
that is disjoint from the union of
\widetilde{\lambda}
and the
\beta_{\widetilde{T}}
and goes from the negative endpoint of
F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1}
to the negative endpoint of
\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
. Then, the chain
F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1}+\widetilde{k}_{+}-\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}-\widetilde{k}_{-}
is homologous to 0, and

c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1})=-c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{+})+c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})+c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{-})=c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})

for each
i=1
,
2
, since
c_{V_{i}}
is closed and
c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{\pm})=0
by Lemma
31
.

Similarly,

c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{2})=c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})

and

c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{1})=c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}}).

Also, the images of the sides
F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{1}
and
F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{2}
are equal to the same edge of the triangulation
\Sigma
, but induce opposite orientations on this edge. It follows that
c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{1})=-c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{2})
for each
i=1
,
2
, since
c_{V_{i}}
is closed. Using twice more the fact that
c_{V_{i}}
is closed,

\displaystyle c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{2})

\displaystyle=c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{2})-c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{2})=-c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{mid}}\widetilde{f}_{1})-c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{2})

\displaystyle=-c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1})-c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{1})-c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{2})

\displaystyle=-c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})-c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}})-c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}).

In the formula of Lemma
38
, the sum of the contributions of
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
to
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
is equal to

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigl(K\bigl(c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1}),c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{1})\bigr)-K\bigl(c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1}),c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{1})\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigl(K\bigl(c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{2}),c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{2})\bigr)-K\bigl(c_{V_{2}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{2}),c_{V_{1}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{2})\bigr)\Bigr).

Substituting the above values for
c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{1})
,
c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{1})
,
c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{front}}\widetilde{f}_{2})
and
c_{V_{i}}(F^{\mathrm{back}}\widetilde{f}_{2})
and rearranging terms gives the formula stated in the lemma.
∎

For
i=1
,
2
, the values of
c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}})
,
c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})
and
c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\in\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
are provided by Lemmas
32
and
33
. However, the formulas of Lemma
33
for
c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})
and
c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})
involve infinite sums, which is not very convenient if we want to input these values in the formula of Lemma
44
. We will consequently use the approximation of §
5.4
, and assume that
\Phi=\Phi_{\delta}
is obtained from a first train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
by opening zippers up to depth
\delta\geqslant 1
.

As before, lift the switch tie
s
of
\Phi
to a switch tie
\widetilde{s}
of the preimage
\widetilde{\Phi}\subset\widetilde{S}
of
\Phi
. The correspondence of Proposition
6
associates a component
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\lambda}
to the component of
\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{\Phi}
that has a corner at
\widetilde{s}
, and also associates a vertex
x_{\widetilde{T}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
of
\widetilde{T}
to that corner. Index the other two vertices of
\widetilde{T}
as
y_{\widetilde{T}}
and
z_{\widetilde{T}}\in\partial_{\infty}\widetilde{S}
so that, as usual,
x_{\widetilde{T}}
,
y_{\widetilde{T}}
and
z_{\widetilde{T}}
occur counterclockwise in this order around
\widetilde{T}
.

Lemma 45
.

Let
\Phi
be obtained from a first train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
by opening zippers up to depth
\delta
. Then, with the above data, there is a constant
C
such that the contribution of the switch
s
to
\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
is of the form

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\bigl(\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})-\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\bigr)K\bigl(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}},\dot{S}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad-\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\bigl(\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(T,x)-\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(T,x)\bigr)K\bigl(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}},\dot{L}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\bigl(\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(T,x)-\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(T,x)\bigr)K\bigl(\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}},\dot{L}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}).

The constant
C>0
and the constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O(\ )
depend only on the train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
, on the Hitchin representation
\rho
and on the tangent vectors
V_{1}
,
V_{2}\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
.

Proof.

Because the Killing form
K(\ ,\ )
is invariant under the adjoint representation, the proposed formula is independent of the lift
\widetilde{s}
of the switch tie
s
. We can therefore assume that
\widetilde{s}
is contained in a compact subset of
\widetilde{S}
depending only on
\Phi_{0}
. Then, Lemmas
32
and
34
provide

\displaystyle c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{switch}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b+c=n}\dot{\tau}^{abc}_{V_{i}}(T,x)\dot{L}^{abc}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}

\displaystyle c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V_{i}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}F_{\widetilde{T}}}+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

\displaystyle c_{V_{i}}(\widetilde{k}_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a+b=n}\dot{\sigma}_{V_{i}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\dot{S}^{ab}_{E_{\widetilde{T}}G_{\widetilde{T}}}+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}),

where the constant
C>0
and the constants hidden in the Landau symbols
O(\ )
depend only on that compact subset, on the Hitchin representation
\rho
and on the tangent vectors
V_{1}
,
V_{2}\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
.

The result then follows by entering these expressions in the formula of Lemma
44
.
∎

The Killing form terms are given by the following formulas.

Lemma 46
.

For every maximum-span flag triple
(E,F,G)\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{3}
,

\displaystyle K\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{EG},\dot{S}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}_{EF}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
2ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}\end{cases}

\displaystyle K\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{EG},\dot{L}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}_{EFG}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
-2a(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}\end{cases}

\displaystyle K\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{EF},\dot{L}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}_{EFG}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
-2a(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Proof.

We use the computations of the proof of Lemma
42
. As in that proof consider, for any maximum-span flag triple
(E,F,G)\in\mathrm{Flag}(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{3}
, the projection

P_{EFG}^{abc}=\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus 0\,\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}.

Then,

\displaystyle\dot{S}_{EF}^{ab}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\tfrac{-a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}=\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{FGE}^{b0a}=\tfrac{-a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+P_{EFG}^{ab0}

\displaystyle\dot{S}_{EG}^{ab}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\tfrac{-a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(b)}}=\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{GEF}^{ba0}=\tfrac{-a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+P_{EFG}^{a0b}

\displaystyle\dot{L}_{EFG}^{abc}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-b-c}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{E^{(a)}}\oplus\tfrac{a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{F^{(b)}}\oplus\tfrac{a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{G^{(c)}}=\tfrac{a}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{EFG}^{abc}.

In the proof of Lemma
42
, we computed that

\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{\beta\gamma\alpha}P_{EFG}^{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\alpha\geqslant\alpha^{\prime}\text{ or }\beta\leqslant\beta^{\prime}\\
\beta-\beta^{\prime}&\text{if }\alpha\leqslant\alpha^{\prime},\ \beta\geqslant\beta^{\prime}\text{ and }\gamma\geqslant\gamma^{\prime}\\
\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha&\text{if }\alpha\leqslant\alpha^{\prime},\ \beta\geqslant\beta^{\prime}\text{ and }\gamma\leqslant\gamma^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Exchanging the roles of
F
and
G
, and noting that
P_{GFE}^{\gamma\beta\alpha}=P_{GEF}^{\gamma\alpha\beta}
and
P_{EGF}^{\alpha^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}=P_{EFG}^{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}
, this also implies that

\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{GEF}^{\gamma\alpha\beta}P_{EFG}^{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\alpha\geqslant\alpha^{\prime}\text{ or }\gamma\leqslant\gamma^{\prime}\\
\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}&\text{if }\alpha\leqslant\alpha^{\prime},\ \gamma\geqslant\gamma^{\prime}\text{ and }\beta\geqslant\beta^{\prime}\\
\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha&\text{if }\alpha\leqslant\alpha^{\prime},\ \gamma\geqslant\gamma^{\prime}\text{ and }\beta\leqslant\beta^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Therefore,

\displaystyle K\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{EG},\dot{S}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}_{EF}\right)

\displaystyle=2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,\left(\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{GEF}^{ba0}\right)\left(\tfrac{-a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}0}\right)

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{2a^{\prime}b}{n}\mathrm{Tr}\,\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+2b\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}0}+2a^{\prime}\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{GEF}^{ba0}-2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{GEF}^{ba0}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}0}

\displaystyle=-2a^{\prime}b+2a^{\prime}b+2a^{\prime}b-2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{GEF}^{ba0}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}0}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
2ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},\end{cases}

using the property that
a+b=a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n
.

Similarly,

\displaystyle K\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{EG},\dot{L}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}_{EFG}\right)

\displaystyle=2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,\left(\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{GEF}^{ba0}\right)\left(\tfrac{a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=2a^{\prime}b-2a^{\prime}b-2a^{\prime}b+2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{GEF}^{ba0}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\text{ or }b\leqslant c^{\prime}\\
-2a(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}\text{ and }b\geqslant c^{\prime}.\end{cases}

This can be simplified by noticing that, if
a\leqslant a^{\prime}
, then necessarily

b=n-a\geqslant n-a^{\prime}>n-a^{\prime}-b^{\prime}=c^{\prime},

so that the second condition is irrelevant. Therefore,

K\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{EG},\dot{L}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}_{EFG}\right)=\begin{cases}-2a^{\prime}(n-a)&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
-2a(n-a^{\prime})&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}.\end{cases}

The same argument gives

\displaystyle K\left(\dot{S}^{ab}_{EF},\dot{L}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}_{EFG}\right)

\displaystyle=2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,\left(\tfrac{b}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{FGE}^{b0a}\right)\left(\tfrac{a^{\prime}}{n}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=2a^{\prime}b-2a^{\prime}b-2a^{\prime}b+2n\,\mathrm{Tr}\,P_{FGE}^{b0a}P_{EFG}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\text{ or }b\leqslant b^{\prime}\\
-2a(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}\text{ and }b\geqslant b^{\prime}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-2a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
-2a(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}.\end{cases}

This concludes the proof.
∎

6.5.
An approximate formula

We can now put everything together.

For a switch
s
of a train track
\Psi
, let
U_{s}
denote the component of
S-\Psi
that has a corner at
s
. Also, arbitrarily choose a corner
s_{U}
(corresponding to a switch of
\Psi
) for each component
U
of
S-\Psi
.

Every oriented branch
e
of
\Psi
and every
a
,
b\geqslant 1
with
a+b=n
, the corresponding generalized Fock-Goncharov invariant defines a function
\sigma^{ab}(e):\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)\to\mathbb{R}
, and therefore a 1–form
d\sigma^{ab}(e)\in\Omega^{1}\big(\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)\big)
. Similarly, every component
U
of
S-\Psi
and every triple of integers
a
,
b
,
c\geqslant 1
with
a+b+c=n
provides another 1–form
d\tau^{abc}(U,s_{U})\in\Omega^{1}\big(\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)\big)
.

Lemma 47
.

Let
\Phi_{\delta}
be obtained from a first train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
of the geodesic lamination
\lambda
by opening zippers up to depth
\delta\geqslant 1
, and let
\Psi_{\delta}
be the train track corresponding to
\Phi_{\delta}
. At each switch
s
of
\Psi_{\delta}
, orient the incoming switches
e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
and
e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}
toward that switch. Then, the Atiyah-Bott-Goldman symplectic form
\omega\in\Omega^{2}\big(\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)\big)
at
[\rho]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
is of the form

\displaystyle\omega_{[\rho]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\text{component}\\
\text{of }S-\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\tfrac{1}{2}C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\,d\tau^{abc}(U,s_{U})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}),

where the constants
C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}
and
C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}
are given by

\displaystyle C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{\prime}b-ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
ac^{\prime}-a^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\\
b^{\prime}c-bc^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
b^{\prime}c-bc^{\prime}&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\\
ac^{\prime}-a^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
a^{\prime}b-ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\end{cases}

\displaystyle C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},\end{cases}

and where the constant
C>0
and the constant hidden in the Landau symbol
O(\ )
depend only on a Hitchin representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(S)\to\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
representing the character
[\rho]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
and on the original train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
V_{1}
,
V_{2}\in T_{[\rho]}\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
be two tangent vectors of the Hitchin component at
[\rho]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
, respectively associated to variations
\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}
,
\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}
and
\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}
,
\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}
of the generalized Fock-Goncharov invariants of
[\rho]
. Lemmas
43
,
45
and
46
then give us that the pairing of
\omega(V_{1},V_{2})=\left\langle[c_{V_{1}}]{\smallsmile}[c_{V_{2}}],[S]\right\rangle
of
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
under the symplectic form
\omega
is of the form

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\text{component}\\
\text{of }S-\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\tfrac{1}{2}C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\left(\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})-\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{abc}(U,s_{U})\,\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\left(\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})-\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\left(\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s)-\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{ab}(e^{\mathrm{left}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s_{U})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi_{\delta}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\left(\dot{\sigma}_{V_{1}}^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{2}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s)-\dot{\sigma}_{V_{2}}^{ab}(e^{\mathrm{right}})\dot{\tau}_{V_{1}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta}),

which is exactly the property that we needed.
∎

We will show that the above estimate is actually an equality, with the error term
O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})
equal to 0.

6.6.
Invariance under zipper opening

For a train track neighborhood
\Phi_{\delta}
of
\lambda
obtained from a first train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
by opening zippers up to depth
\delta
, Lemma
47
estimates the symplectic form
\omega
at
[\rho]\in\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
as

\omega_{[\rho]}=A(\Phi_{\delta})+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

where, for any train track neighborhood
\Phi
of
\lambda
with associated train track
\Psi
,

\displaystyle A(\Phi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\text{component}\\
\text{of }S-\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\tfrac{1}{2}C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\,d\tau^{abc}(U,s_{U})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s).

We show that this approximation is invariant under zipper opening.

Lemma 48
.

If the train track neighborhood
\Phi^{\prime}
is obtained from
\Phi
by a zipper opening along an arc
a
, then

A(\Phi^{\prime})=A(\Phi).

Proof.

By definition of zipper openings, the arc
a
starts at a switch point, is disjoint from the geodesic lamination, and is transverse to the ties of
\Phi
. If
a
is disjoint from the switch ties of
\Phi
(except at its initial point), then the combinatorial structure of the associated train track is unchanged, and
A(\Phi^{\prime})=A(\Phi)
.

We can therefore assume, without loss of generality, that
a
crosses the switch ties of
\Phi
in exactly one point. Then, there are several cases to consider, according to the way
a
crosses that switch tie. The upshot is that the train tracks
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
respectively associated to
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
differ by one of the moves of Figures
14
and
15
or their mirror images.

s_{1}

s_{2}

e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{left}}

e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{right}}

e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{out}}=e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{out}}

e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{right}}

e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{left}}

s_{1}^{\prime}

s_{2}^{\prime}

e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}}

e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{out}}

e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}}=e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}}

e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{out}}

e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}}

Figure 14.
Opening zippers I

s_{1}

s_{2}

e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{right}}

e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{out}}

e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{left}}=e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{out}}

e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{right}}

e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{left}}

s_{1}^{\prime}

s_{2}^{\prime}

e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}}

e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{out}}

e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{out}}=e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}}

e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}}

e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}}

Figure 15.
Opening zippers II

We first look at the case of Figure
14
. Then,
A(\Phi)
and
A(\Phi^{\prime})
differ only in the contribution of the two switches
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
appearing in the picture. The contribution to
A(\Phi)
of the switches
s_{1}
,
s_{2}
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{right}})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{right}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{1}},s_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}},s_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{right}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{1}},s_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{right}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}},s_{2}),

and the contribution of
s_{1}^{\prime}
and
s_{2}^{\prime}
to
A(\Phi^{\prime})
is obtained from the same formula by replacing
s_{1}
by
s_{1}^{\prime}
and
s_{2}
by
s_{2}^{\prime}
everywhere.

Note that, for every
a
,
b
,
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
,
c^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{1}},s_{1})

\displaystyle=\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}},s_{2})

\displaystyle=\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{left}})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})

\displaystyle\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{left}})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}}),

and

\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}})=\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}})

since we orient the left/right branches toward the associated switch; in particular, the branches
e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}}
and
e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}}
coincide but come with opposite orientations. Also, by the Switch Condition of §
1.3
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{right}})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{out}})=\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})+\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1\\
b^{\prime\prime}+c^{\prime\prime}=n-b\end{subarray}}\tau^{bb^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})+\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1\\
b^{\prime\prime}+c^{\prime\prime}=n-b\end{subarray}}\tau^{bb^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})

and, similarly,

\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{right}})=\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})+\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{right}})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1\\
b^{\prime\prime}+c^{\prime\prime}=n-b\end{subarray}}\tau^{bb^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime}).

Combining all these observations and comparing contributions term by term, we can now express
A(\Phi)-A(\Phi^{\prime})
as

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge\bigg(d\sigma^{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1\\
b^{\prime\prime}+c^{\prime\prime}=n-b^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\,d\tau^{b^{\prime}b^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge\bigg(d\sigma^{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1\\
b^{\prime\prime}+c^{\prime\prime}=n-b^{\prime}\end{subarray}}d\tau^{b^{\prime}b^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\bigg(d\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})-\sum_{b^{\prime\prime}+c^{\prime\prime}=n-b}d\tau^{bb^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})\bigg)\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\bigg(d\sigma^{ba}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})-\sum_{b^{\prime\prime}+c^{\prime\prime}=n-b}d\tau^{bb^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})\bigg)\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})

Expanding the above expression and renaming indices, this is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})a^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}_{ba}\,d\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})a^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ba}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{2}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})a^{\prime}}_{a(b+c)}\,d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})\wedge d\tau^{abc}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ba}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s_{1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c=n\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{(b+c)a}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}d\tau^{abc}(U_{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{1}^{\prime})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s_{2}^{\prime}},s_{2}^{\prime}).

From the definition of the constant
C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}
in Lemma
47
, we have that
C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}=C^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}_{ab}=C^{ba}_{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}
for every
a
,
b
,
a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
. In particular,
C^{ab}_{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}=C^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}_{ba}
and the first and third line therefore cancel out.

Similarly,
C^{ab}_{(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})a^{\prime}}=C^{ba}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}
and the second line cancels out with the seventh, while the fourth line cancels out with the fifth.

Finally,
C^{(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})a^{\prime}}_{a(b+c)}=C^{(b+c)a}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}
and the sixth line cancels out with the eighth.

Therefore,
A(\Phi)=A(\Phi^{\prime})
when
\Psi^{\prime}
is obtained from
\Psi
by the move of Figure
14
.

A very similar argument shows that
A(\Phi)=A(\Phi^{\prime})
when
\Psi^{\prime}
is obtained from
\Psi
by the move of Figure
15
. Exchanging “left” and “right” and reversing signs everywhere also proves invariance under the mirror images of these two moves.
∎

7.
Proof of the main theorem

We are now ready to prove our main theorem, namely Theorem
1
of the Introduction. With the notation of §
1.3
and subsequent sections, this is the following statement.

Theorem 49
.

Let
\lambda
be a maximal geodesic lamination in the closed oriented surface
S
, and let
\Psi
be a train track carrying
\lambda
. At each switch
s
of
\Psi
, orient the incoming switches
e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}}
and
e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}}
toward that switch. Then, the Atiyah-Bott-Goldman symplectic form
\omega
of the Hitchin component
\mathrm{Hit}_{n}(S)
can be expressed in terms of the generalized Fock-Goncharov coordinate functions
\tau^{abc}(U,s)
and
\sigma^{ab}(b)
as

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\text{component}\\
\text{of }S-\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c\geqslant 1\\
a+b+c=n\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{\prime},b^{\prime},c^{\prime}\geqslant 1\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}\tfrac{1}{2}C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\,d\tau^{abc}(U,s_{U})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U,s_{U})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\geqslant 1\\
a+b=n\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\geqslant 1\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\sigma^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\geqslant 1\\
a+b=n\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{\prime},b^{\prime},c^{\prime}\geqslant 1\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{left}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\text{ switch}\\
\text{of }\Psi\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\geqslant 1\\
a+b=n\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{\prime},b^{\prime},c^{\prime}\geqslant 1\\
a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}+c^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}(b^{\prime}+c^{\prime})}\,d\sigma^{ab}(e_{s}^{\mathrm{right}})\wedge d\tau^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(U_{s},s).

with

\displaystyle C^{abc}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{\prime}b-ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
ac^{\prime}-a^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\\
b^{\prime}c-bc^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
b^{\prime}c-bc^{\prime}&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\\
ac^{\prime}-a^{\prime}c&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\leqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\geqslant c^{\prime}\\
a^{\prime}b-ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime},b\geqslant b^{\prime}\text{ and }c\leqslant c^{\prime}\end{cases}

\displaystyle C^{ab}_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{\prime}b&\text{if }a\geqslant a^{\prime}\\
ab^{\prime}&\text{if }a\leqslant a^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
\Phi_{\delta}
be obtained from the train track neighborhood
\Phi_{0}
corresponding to
\Psi
by opening zippers up to depth
\delta
.
Lemma
47
and
48
show that

\omega=A(\Phi_{\delta})+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})=A(\Phi_{0})+O(\mathrm{e}^{-C\delta})

for some constant
C>0
. Letting
\delta
tend to infinity proves that the approximation is actually an equality.
∎
